1c:T183b,Refs #30744, #30825

I found three defects while verifying the merged desktop fixes on a packaged macOS build (unsigned Electrobun build from origin/develop `279e585dd84`, port 31337 held by a dummy listener). One commit each.

## 1. Local backups fail on every desktop and standalone agent (`fix(agent)`)

`POST /api/backups` returns HTTP 500 even for a tiny PGlite agent:
- The local backup path calls `createKmsClient()` with no options.
- Outside tests, that resolves to the Steward backend. A local agent never configures Steward.
- The snapshot manifest is built, then encryption throws:

```
Info  [agent-backup] Snapshot manifest created (database=pglite-dump, mediaFiles=0, vaultFiles=0, stateFiles=32)
Error [agent-backup] Local backup failed (err=ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward requires steward.{baseUrl, tokenProvider})
```

The suites missed it because `NODE_ENV=test` selects the in-memory KMS. The v1 envelope and the streamed v2 key wrap are both affected.

**Fix:** local backups now seal with the local KMS adapter, rooted in the same OS keychain master key as the agent vault (or `ELIZA_VAULT_PASSPHRASE`).
- An explicit `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND`, `ELIZA_LOCAL_MODE`, `ELIZA_LOCAL_ROOT_KEY`, or a test environment keeps the current resolution.
- An unavailable master key is surfaced and not cached.
- Limits and backup contents are unchanged.

## 2. Tray, menu, shortcut and deep-link callbacks crash the packaged app (`fix(desktop)`)

Bun 1.4.2 (the bundled runtime) passes `FFIType.cstring` JSCallback arguments as JS strings. Electrobun 1.18.1 converts each one with `new CString(arg)`, which throws `TypeError: ptr must be a number`. I reproduced this with a standalone Bun FFI callback.

On the packaged app:
- Every `webviewEvent` / `eventBridgeHandler` conversion logs this error and the event is dropped.
- The tray, application-menu, context-menu, global-shortcut, URL-open (deep link) and MIME callbacks have no try/catch. The first tray action or Cmd+Q ended in `Uncaught exception in worker` and killed the main process.
- The embedded agent was left orphaned (ppid 1) on port 31339.

**Fix:** the desktop build now rewrites these callback conversions in the shared Electrobun source and in every downloaded platform core (`dist-*`). The rewrite sits next to the existing RPC socket hardening, and the packaging step repackages when the downloaded core changes. The helper accepts strings, null, or raw pointers. If the upstream code shape changes, the build fails.

After the fix, the same tray and menu Quit actions run the normal shutdown: `App quitting (explicit-quit)`, agent stopped, no crash.

## 3. A returning packaged profile never leaves `starting-runtime` (`fix(desktop)`)

The packaged desktop embeds the app server host (`packages/app/src/runtime/startup-state.ts`). Its `/api/health` reports `startup.phase` as `runtime-ready`, `features-starting`, `ready` or `degraded`, all projected as agent state `running`. Only the legacy agent host reports `running`.

Two checks required the phase to be exactly `running`:
- The shell's launch orchestrator (`classifyPhase`).
- The renderer's `isRuntimeReadyFromBootProgress` and `isRuntimeReadyFromLaunchProgress`.

So with a configured profile:
- The launch snapshot stayed at `agent-api-ready` (`boot.runtimePhase: "ready"`).
- The renderer stayed in `starting-runtime` with an empty pill window, even though `/api/status` said `running`.

Fresh profiles were unaffected because first-run gating runs first. Dev and web builds were unaffected because they don't use the desktop RPC.

**Fix:** both checks accept every phase the hosts project as running.

## Verification

Packaged app, unsigned, 31337 occupied by a dummy listener. The embedded API moved to 31339 in every run.

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| Fresh state dir | Backend `first-run/status complete:false`, `status not_started (awaiting-onboarding)`. Renderer: `restoring-session → resolving-target → polling-backend → first-run-required`, showing "Let's get you signed in / Sign in to Eliza Cloud" with a gated composer. Never ready. |
| Returning profile, before fix 3 | `/api/status running`, but the renderer stayed in `starting-runtime` with an empty body for more than 4 minutes. |
| Returning profile, after fix 3 | `starting-runtime → hydrating → ready`. `meta.firstRunComplete` preserved and `first-run/status complete:true`. |
| Backup round trip, packaged app after fix 1 | Created conversation `PACKAGED-BACKUP-MARKER`, then `POST /api/backups` (200, 82 MB), then deleted it, then `POST /api/backups/restore` (`{"restored":true,"requiresRestart":true}`). After relaunch the marker is back. |
| Backup round trip, standalone agent, before and after fix 1 | Before: 500 and no file written. After: 200, and the restore brings back the deleted conversation and drops the one added after the backup. |

1d:T117a,## Routine delivery with no conversation (Refs #31022)

A fresh agent with no conversation dropped fired in-app routines, but the scheduler still recorded them as delivered. Two things caused it:

- The personal-assistant `in_app` dispatcher counted an `assistant` event emit as delivery. That emit is fire-and-forget.
- The server's autonomy router, `routeAutonomyTextToUser`, returned silently when no conversation existed.

### Fix
This uses option (a): resolve or create the owner's canonical conversation, then persist durably.

- **core**: Adds the internal send source `owner_chat` (`MESSAGE_SOURCE_OWNER_CHAT`). `sendMessageToTarget` now throws a typed `ElizaError` with the code `SEND_HANDLER_NOT_FOUND` when a source has no handler. The message text is unchanged.
- **agent**: Registers an `owner_chat` handler that picks the owner's canonical conversation:
  - It uses the active conversation, else the most recent one. It never picks a wallet-scoped waifu conversation.
  - If none exists, `ensureOwnerConversation` creates one through the same room setup as `POST /api/conversations`, and concurrent creations share one result.

  The handler persists the message before it resolves. A `deliveryIdempotencyKey` derives the message id, so a replay returns the stored row instead of writing a second one.
- **personal-assistant**: `in_app` dispatch now awaits the durable history write.
  - Only chat history or the notification inbox count as delivery. The event bus does not.
  - The emitted event carries the persisted `roomId`, so the autonomy router does not store a second copy.
  - If the history write fails, the dispatch returns a typed, retryable `transport_error`.
  - A headless host has no `owner_chat` handler and no notifier, so it gets `disconnected` and the task is never marked delivered.
  - `owner_chat` is added to the internal-source set in `delivery-binding.ts`, which checks for missing sources at compile time.

### Test
`packages/agent/test/scheduled-routine-no-conversation-delivery.test.ts` runs on a real SQLite runtime with the scheduling plugin, the PA production dispatcher, the agent event bus and the HTTP server:
- **Headless** (no server): firing the routine records `lastDispatchResult` `{ok:false, reason:"disconnected"}`, and the fire result is not `fired`. On develop this assertion fails, because the fire result is `fired`.
- **Server running, no conversation, no client**: the routine fires. When a client connects, `GET /api/conversations` returns exactly one conversation, and its room matches `ownerChatRoomId` in the scheduler record. Its history holds the routine exactly once, under the `ownerChatMessageId` the scheduler recorded.
- **Replay** of the same occurrence: dispatch returns the same message id, with no new row and no new conversation.

1f:T116e,Refs #31017. This is the follow-up to #32870. It fixes the remaining action-retrieval problems at their source.

## (b) Incidental domain words no longer load whole families

In `convo.echo-self-test`, Stage 1 selects `ECHO_TEST` for "Please echo this message back to me: hello world". The `world` token matched the MESSAGE family's `world` context, so the pending-intent bootstrap loaded nine `MESSAGE_*` tools. `pendingActionContexts` in `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/services/message/action-surface.ts` now requires a stronger signal when a clause is already claimed by a selected action (its verb names that action, e.g. `echo` names `ECHO_TEST`). An incidental domain word in that clause counts only if the clause also names one of the domain's operations. Otherwise the family stays behind `DISCOVER_ACTIONS`. Unclaimed clauses keep the existing inference, including #32835's pending programming-work cases. Declared contexts and explicit discovery are unchanged.

## (a) Promoted children no longer repeat the parent description

`promoteSubactionsToActions` used to compose each virtual's description as `${parent.description} — subaction = x`. A virtual now states only its operation, for example `MESSAGE operation "edit".` plus any authored override. The promotion marker carries the umbrella description. `buildPlannerToolsFromActions` and `buildPlannerToolsFromTieredActions` state it once per tool list: on the family's first exposed operation, with later operations referencing that tool. When the umbrella tool is already on the wire they add nothing. Canonical alias contracts (`planned-tool.ts`) and sub-planner value guides reconstruct the exact prior composed text from the marker. No context is truncated or summarized; the umbrella text is stated once rather than N times.

20:T857,## Problem
`cloud / e2e-tests` on develop failed 3× in a row at e1de9b114d (run 36392903797). Each time the worker "died" 14–20 s into the suite on a different route (MCP, discord heartbeat, advertising media): a plain-text 500 `Error: Network connection lost.` from Miniflare's entry worker, or wrangler exiting with an empty `✘ [ERROR]`. No `packages/cloud` code changed between the last green run and this SHA.

## Root cause
`wrangler dev` forwards every request ProxyWorker → UserWorker over a pooled keep-alive connection; both kj endpoints close idle sockets after 5 s, so a request written at that boundary never reaches the Worker. Wrangler only retries GET/HEAD, and the test client's `Connection: close` cannot reach that hop (cloudflare/workers-sdk#14641). The race is always present (passing runs also log kj `Broken pipe`) and becomes fatal under runner load. Reproduced on stock wrangler 4.116.0 with a hello-world Worker in a Linux container: 673–1286 of 1920 timed requests failed, then the server died with the same empty `[ERROR]`.

## Fix
Test lanes (`CLOUD_E2E=1`, `NODE_ENV=test`, no custom wrangler args) launch the Worker through `wrangler-direct-dev.ts`. It keeps wrangler's config, bundler and Miniflare runtime (`unstable_DevEnv`), but serves the public port from a Node listener that never idles out clients and forwards each request to the UserWorker over a fresh connection with the same original-URL and shared-secret headers. It never replays requests and rejects any unrecognized flag. Local dev is unchanged.

No assertions were weakened, no tests were skipped, and no retries were added.

## Verification
- Linux A/B burst test: 0/1920 failures with the launcher vs 673 and 1286 on stock wrangler. JSON, 100 KB and 5 MB bodies, multiple set-cookies, SSE and WebSocket upgrades behave the same as stock.
- `bun run test:cloud:e2e` against local Postgres, run twice: 18/18 files, 512 pass / 19 skip, identical to the baseline.
- `bun run test:cloud:integration`: 511 pass, 0 fail. No leftover workerd processes.
- biome clean.

22:Td2a,## Fixes #32880

`projectFinalizedTranscriptCommitments` / `commitmentRecordsFromTranscript` resolved `tomorrow`, weekdays, and explicit dates on the UTC calendar and pinned deadlines at 17:00 UTC. An evening promise in a zone behind UTC was a day late, and it disagreed with the zone-aware meeting-ghost row (#31249 / #32837) written by the same finalized event.

**Changes (plugin-personal-assistant)**
- `transcript-hook.ts`: resolves the owner's effective zone at the transcript instant with `resolveOwnerTimeZone`. This is the same resolver and instant the meeting-ghost consumer uses. The zone is passed into the extractor.
- `commitments/ledger.ts`: `extractCommitmentLedgerRecords` accepts an optional `timeZone` and uses the shared `lifeops/time` local-day helpers (`getZonedDateParts`, `addDaysToLocalDate`, `getWeekdayForLocalDate`, `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`) for explicit dates, `tomorrow`, weekdays, and the 17:00 local wall time. Callers that pass no zone (chat, sent mail) keep UTC behavior. An impossible explicit date such as `2026-02-30` now gives no deadline instead of rolling into the next month.
- `meeting-ghost/index.ts`: aligns the two paths so one spoken commitment never persists as two rows with conflicting deadlines. The two writers used different source keys (`transcriptId:segmentId` vs `meetingId:commitmentId`), so both rows persisted.
  - Relative days now count from the utterance instant (meeting start + `segment.startMs`), which matches the projection's `observedAt`.
  - The ghost analyzer no longer writes a ledger row for the owner's own first-person promise when the transcript projection writes that segment. Named commitments ("Ava will…") spoken by the owner, and every other speaker's commitments, still come from the ghost path. Approvals and calendar intents are unchanged.

**Regression** (`test/meeting-ghost.integration.test.ts`, real PGlite runtime through `handleMeetingTranscriptFinalized`):
- The issue's exact repro: America/Los_Angeles, Monday 2026-09-14 17:30, "I will send the report tomorrow." gives `transcript-local-day:seg-1` with `dueAt` `2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z` and `observedAt` `2026-09-15T00:30:00.000Z`. 24:T1e98,# Relates to

#32905 added the durable cutover hold (#22934), and it merged today. `drainCutoverHolds` promises that "a per-hold lease keeps replicas from running the same turn concurrently", but that only holds while a redelivery finishes within one lease period.

`packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/cutover-hold.ts` on develop does three things:

- It takes `SET NX EX 180` on `webhook:cutover-hold-lease:<dedupKey>`.
- It awaits `handlers.redeliver(held)`. That is `redeliverHeldWebhook` → `processMessage` → `sendPersonalSharedReply`, which runs a full Personal Shared turn: up to `PERSONAL_SHARED_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS = 15 * 60_000` per attempt (`webhook-handler.ts:78`), with up to `PERSONAL_SHARED_ATTEMPTS = 3` attempts.
- It always runs `finally { await redis.del(leaseKey) }`.

The hold stays due in `webhook:cutover-holds` while it is being redelivered, and every replica polls every 2 s (`index.ts:256`, `CUTOVER_HOLD_DRAIN_INTERVAL_MS = 2_000`). `draining` only stops new drains, so during a rolling deploy the old pod keeps its in-flight redelivery while the new pod polls. So:

1. **Duplicate turn:** once a redelivery runs past 180 s, another replica re-acquires the lease and delivers the same held turn again.
2. **Lease stolen on release:** the first drainer's unconditional `del` then deletes the second drainer's lease, so a third can join.

`redeliverHeldWebhook` calls `processMessage` directly, so the ingress-side processing claim does not protect this path. The user receives the reply twice. On private Blooio/Twilio replies the gateway has no egress lease.

# Change

`drainCutoverHolds` (`cutover-hold.ts`):

- The lease value is a per-drain `randomUUID()` token.
  - Before, it was `String(now)`, and the Redis adapters JSON-parse reads, so that value came back as a number.
- While the redelivery runs, the lease is renewed every third of its period. Renewal stops, with a warning, if the lease is no longer ours. A failed renewal is logged and retried on the next tick.
- The lease is released only if we still own it.
- `options.leaseSeconds` (default 180, unchanged) lets tests outlive a lease in real time.
- **Crash recovery is unchanged:** a dead drainer's lease still lapses within one period.
- Renewal and release are atomic compare-and-act operations: `GatewayRedis.expireIfEquals` / `delIfEquals`, each a single server-side Lua script on the ioredis, ioredis-mock and Upstash adapters.
  - A GET-then-act check (the first revision) left a one-round-trip gap in which the lease could change hands. Thanks @ss251.
  - The hand-written `MemoryRedis` fake in `personal-private-egress.test.ts` implements the two methods too.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop`.
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `3c2fbd0a5e`.

**Reproduced on develop (unmodified).**
- A throwaway test (not committed) drove the real `handleWebhook` → hold → `drainCutoverHolds` → `redeliverHeldWebhook` path against a local Cloud route, with the Blooio provider API substituted.
- The Cloud route deletes the lease key while the attested turn runs, to stand in for the 180 s lease lapsing.
- A second drainer then delivered the same turn:

```
first {"delivered":1,...} second {"delivered":1,...} cloudTurns 2 providerSends 2
(fail) develop: a lapsed lease lets a second replica deliver the same held turn
```

**New tests** in `__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts` use the same real handler, drainer and Redis path as the existing cutover tests (in-memory Redis; only the Blooio API is substituted):

- *a redelivery that outlives one lease period is not run again by another replica*:
  - Setup: lease is 1 s, the attested turn takes 2.5 s, and a second drainer runs at 1.5 s.
  - Expected: the second drain is a no-op, and there is exactly 1 new Cloud turn, 1 provider send, and ledger `delivered`.
- *a drainer releases only its own lease*:
  - Setup: another replica's token replaces the lease mid-turn.
  - Expected: finishing leaves it intact. **Fails on develop** (`Expected: "other-replica-lease"`, the lease was deleted).

A new contract test runs `delIfEquals` / `expireIfEquals` through the in-memory adapter, which executes the real Lua scripts. It checks that a lost lease cannot extend or delete another owner's lease, while the owner can do both.

`bun test __tests__/` (gateway-webhook) on this head: **14 pass, 0 fail across 4 files**. Each lease test fails when its half of the fix is removed:

| Sabotage | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Renewal disabled | "outlives one lease period" fails (a second delivery happens); the others pass |
| Unconditional `del(leaseKey)` restored | "releases only its own lease" fails; the others pass |

Also run: `tsc --noEmit` (gateway-webhook) passes, and `biome check` on both files is clean.

# Change

Preserve the validated operation structurally. After existing caller/turn checks and unique visible-target resolution, known planning selects the registered promoted SHOW child. Known unresolved navigation retains both registered LIST and SHOW without executing either. Parent-only hosts keep the umbrella fallback.

Promotion provenance and discriminator checks prevent an unrelated same-name action from becoming the hint. Authorization, conditions, ordering, domain reads and delivery checks remain unchanged; no generic synonym or retrieval change.

26:T2665,# Relates to

#30330 (merged) shipped four behaviour lines whose tests pass with the lines
deleted; attentionhead's review there documented this. #30376 repaired those
tests and was closed in the 09-26 triage because its files moved. This is the
fresh PR against the current layout that the close note invited.

The old test files (`action-tiering.test.ts`, 12-case
`client-cloud-handoff-target.test.ts`) were removed on 09-23 in `55f4f25689`.
This PR does not restore them. It adds **one case per guard, four in total**,
and nothing else: no shared helpers, no matrix, and no `vi.mock`/`vi.fn`/`vi.spyOn`.

# Change

Test-only. Two new files; no source is touched.

| Guard on develop (`4a657d88e2`) | What it protects | Case |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `left.result.rank - right.result.rank \|\|` in `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/runtime/action-tiering.ts:132` | For parents whose relevance saturates at 1, the planner sees retrieval's order, not alphabetical order. Retrieval's own comments cite live trajectories where a saturated tie decided the surface. | `orders a saturated tie by retrieval rank, not by name` |
| `if (!dedicatedAgentId && sourceIsSharedAdapter) return null;` in `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts` | A shared source with no provisioned target is never switched in place, even when an older control plane omits `executionTier`. | `refuses a shared source with no dedicated target, …` |
| `if (agent.execution_tier === "shared") return null;` | A running row that is shared-tier is never a migration target. | `refuses a running target whose control-plane row is shared-tier` |
| `if (isDirectCloudSharedAgentBase(base) && !isLocalDedicatedProxy) return null;` | A target that advertises the shared REST adapter as its runtime is never switched onto. | `refuses a target that advertises the shared REST adapter as its runtime` |

How the cases are built:

- **Tiering.** The case drives the real `buildActionCatalog`, then
  `retrieveActions`, then `tierActionResults`. It first asserts the input is
  a genuine saturated tie ordered against the alphabet: both scores are 1 and
  WEATHER ranks above ARCHIVE. Then it asserts the tiered order keeps that
  ranking.
- **Handoff.** The cases run the real `ElizaClient.startCloudAgentHandoff`
  against a loopback HTTP server. The server answers the agent detail route,
  runtime health, the shared conversation, and import. So every route a
  completed switch needs is available, and the guard is the only thing
  preventing a switch. Each case asserts:
  - the handoff times out;
  - `onSwitch` is never called;
  - the target runtime is never contacted (no health probe, no import).
- **Deciding rejections without a clock race.** This was the 09-14 backlog
  point on #30376. Polls are sequential, so three detail reads prove two
  handled refusals. A slow runner can only make the test fail; it cannot make
  it pass without the guard. The short-circuit case is the deliberate
  negative: it refuses before any read, so it asserts zero reads across the
  whole budget. No fake timers are needed, because the handoff now accepts
  `intervalMs` and `timeoutMs`.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop`; branched from `origin/develop` at `4a657d88e2`.
- [x] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify`; results below.
- [x] Documentation: N/A. Only test files change.

Test-only gate:

- **Realistic regression prevented.** Deleting any one of the four lines
  leaves every suite on develop green. Two concrete effects: the planner's
  parent order for a saturated tie turns alphabetical, and a cloud handoff
  switches the app onto a base that cannot host a dedicated runtime, which the
  user sees as a dead post-handoff chat.
- **Observing consumer.** For tiering, the tier-A parent order feeds
  `services/message/action-surface.ts` and the planner prompt. For the guards,
  `startCloudAgentHandoff` decides the base the app switches to after
  onboarding and upgrade.
- **Why existing coverage does not own it.** The only surviving tiering test
  (`tasks-issue-similes.test.ts`) never produces a tied score. The cloud
  end-to-end specs cover "no container reachable, so stay" and the success
  switch; neither covers "a shared runtime is reachable, so refuse it". The
  mutation results below demonstrate the gap rather than asserting it.

Results:

- Mutation run at this head. Each line is removed alone, the owning file is
  run, and the source is restored. Each removal fails exactly its own case.
- On Node 24.15.0 with Bun 1.3.14, the new UI file passed 10 of 10 repeated
  runs.

Updates `drizzle-orm` from 0.44.7 to 0.45.3
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/releases">drizzle-orm's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>0.45.3</h2>
<h2>New Netlify DB Driver</h2>
<blockquote>
<p>Note: The Netlify DB driver is developed and maintained by the Netlify team.</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Installation:</strong></p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>npm i @netlify/db
</code></pre>
<p><strong>Usage example:</strong></p>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';
<p>// reads NETLIFY_DB_URL and NETLIFY_DB_DRIVER env vars
const db = drizzle();</p>
<p>const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre></p>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';

const db = drizzle(process.env.DATABASE_URL);

const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';

// Explicit client — consumer controls the driver
const db = drizzle({ client: netlifyDbClient });

const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre>
<h2>0.45.2</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed <code>sql.identifier()</code>, <code>sql.as()</code> escaping issues. Previously all the values passed to this functions were not properly escaped
causing a possible SQL Injection (CWE-89) vulnerability</li>
</ul>
<p>Thanks to <a href="https://github.com/EthanKim88"><code>@​EthanKim88</code></a>, <a href="https://github.com/0x90sh"><code>@​0x90sh</code></a> and <a href="https://github.com/wgoodall01"><code>@​wgoodall01</code></a> for reaching out to us with a reproduction and suggested fix</p>
<h2>0.45.1</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions breaking in environments with forbidden <code>require()</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/5107">#5107</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>0.45.0</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions</li>
<li>Allowed subqueries in select fields</li>
<li>Updated typo algorythm =&gt; algorithm</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

<li><a href="https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/commit/9bd8d464e6328c567c2dbcd8fdd97...

_Description has been truncated_28:Td09,# Relates to

The evaluator could finish a request without an answer even when its decision state required one, causing a reply-writing planner call and another evaluation.

# Change

Require `messageToUser` in the stable output schema and use an empty string for continuation or approval of an existing reply. Existing nonempty-answer checks and legacy parsing stay intact. No model defaults, tool execution rules or restoration behavior change.

2a:T12d3,## #31316: Cloud Worker startup CPU (Refs #29024)

Fixes #31316

### Why it regressed
#31317 moved the Cloud logger and two Shared runtime helpers onto core leaf exports. Then 1047e6d0096 collapsed core's `exports` map to `.` only, so those imports went back to the full `@elizaos/core` root. That commit consolidated the export surface; it did not say the leaves were wrong. Core now has explicit leaf subpaths again, but these files never moved back to them.

The metafile showed a second path that #31317 never removed. `db/schemas/index.ts` imports the table objects from the `@elizaos/plugin-sql` root, and that root imports the core barrel. So fixing only the logger would have left the core barrel in startup. Nine eagerly loaded modules imported the core root. This PR removes all of them.

### Change
- **core**: added the `./security/redact`, `./network/ssrf`, `./types/primitives` and `./utils/string-to-uuid` subpaths.
  - `stringToUuid` moved into its own leaf. `utils.ts` re-exports it.
  - `mintInferenceTraceId` moved into the existing `inference-trace` leaf. `inference-timing.ts` re-exports it.
  - Each function still has one implementation and the root barrel's API is unchanged. The leaves don't import the runtime barrel, as `packages/core/AGENTS.md` requires.
- **plugin-sql**: added a `./schema` entry: a tsup entry plus an export map entry. `src/schema.ts` now also exports its tables by name. It imports only drizzle and core types.
- **cloud/shared**: moved the eager importers to leaves:
  - `logger.ts`, `shared-runtime-channel.ts` and `shared-runtime-history-policy.ts` (the three from the issue)
  - `http-telemetry.ts`, `anonymous-sessions.ts`, `onboarding-coordinator-transport.ts` and `shared-runtime-errors.ts`
  - `db/schemas/index.ts`, which now imports from `@elizaos/plugin-sql/schema`

**What stays the same after 1047e6d0096.** There is still a single cloud logger module and a single redaction policy: `isSensitiveKeyName` and `redactLogArgs` come from the same core `security/redact` module. That module holds no configurable state, only a regex cache. `ElizaError` identity also holds across copies, because `instanceof` checks a shared brand. The deferred alarm-recovery and X-credential imports from #31317 are unchanged.

### Measurements
Same tree and toolchain, from `wrangler deploy --env production --dry-run` (the `check:worker-bundle` script) with `--metafile`. "Eager" means statically reachable from `src/index.ts`, which is what esbuild evaluates at Worker startup.

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Total upload (whole bundle) | 27959.21 KiB / gzip 6903.47 KiB | 27965.13 KiB / gzip 6905.95 KiB |
| Eager modules at startup | 1159 | **483** |
| Eager input bytes | 9.34 MB | **3.11 MB** |
| Core modules evaluated at startup | 487 (4762 KiB) | 0 dist barrel modules; only leaf sources |
| plugin-sql evaluated at startup | 676 KiB (adapter runtime) | 79 KiB (tables only) |
| Core root barrel eager | yes | **no** |

Total bundle size is flat because the heavy code still ships and loads lazily on demand. The gain is in what the Worker evaluates when it starts.

**Startup profile.** I ran `wrangler check startup --worker <prebuilt bundle>` three times on each bundle, interleaved:

| | run 1 | run 2 | run 3 | median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| before, active CPU (ms) | 11716 | 10841 | 13546 | **11716** |
| after, active CPU (ms) | 5638 | 8353 | 5697 | **5697** |
| before, script (ms) | 8955 | 8638 | 10618 | 8955 |
| after, script (ms) | 4962 | 4788 | 4366 | 4788 |

The machine's load average was 150–260 during these runs, so the absolute numbers are far above real Cloudflare timings. Only the relative drop (about 50% active CPU, about 47% script time) means anything. This is local evidence only: canonical staging deployment and the separate Dedicated latency measurement from the issue's acceptance criteria are still pending.

### Verification
- `packages/cloud/api`: `bun run typecheck` (tsc, router contract and `check:worker-bundle` dry run) passed.
- `packages/cloud/shared`: `bun run typecheck` passed.
- `packages/core`: `bun run typecheck` passed.
- `plugins/plugin-sql`: `bun run typecheck` and `lint:check` passed.

2e:T8a3,When a configured remote backend's startup status probe fails, the startup flow skipped the readiness wait and announced `AGENT_RUNNING`. This follow-up to #32862 treats an unavailable status as unproven readiness: it uses the existing bounded polling path, recovers when the backend starts, and presents the existing timeout state if it stays unavailable.

Refs #30744. This fixes the reproduced status-probe failure only. It does not claim packaged macOS or microphone acceptance.

**Status probe classification** (`readRemoteRuntimeState` now uses the existing `runStartupProbe`):
- `running`: immediate-ready path (unchanged).
- HTTP 404, meaning the backend does not expose `/api/status`: keeps the previous immediate-ready path. This addresses @mtcow's review point that self-hosted backends without the endpoint must not regress into `agent-timeout`.
- Transport or other failure, or a response with no `state`: enters the bounded readiness loop.
- Any other reported state: enters the readiness loop (unchanged).

**Tests** (`startup-embedded-first-run.test.tsx`):
- The retry test now asserts that `getStatus` is called twice.
- New coverage for the 404 carve-out.
- New coverage for the persistent-failure timeout.

**Verification** at `2058d2c6e5f` (fix `c573c3d3cfc`, with current develop merged cleanly):
- Focused startup suite: 9/9 passed; with the Android cloud-auth suite, 15/15. Ran with a temporary config override (`pool: threads`, `execArgv: []`) because fork workers stall on this host.
- `packages/ui` typecheck: passed.
- Biome check on `src scripts`: passed. No errors; the warnings are pre-existing and in other files.
- [Full CI](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/36367401896): 29/29 passed. Quality needed 2 reruns after runner shutdowns (exit 143).
- PR Validation: passed.

2f:T7ac,`cloud / e2e-tests` failed on all four attempts of develop `e1de9b114d` (run 36392903797). `cloud / integration-tests` fails intermittently the same way (jobs 108789267585, 108762904596). Each time a different POST or PATCH got a plain-text 500 `Error: Network connection lost.` from Miniflare's `entry.worker.js`.

**Root cause (upstream, still open):** cloudflare/workers-sdk#14641. Wrangler dev's ProxyWorker reuses a pooled connection to the UserWorker at the instant KJ's 5s idle keep-alive timeout closes it. The server closes that idle socket before reading the new request, so the request never reaches the Worker. Our wrangler logs show no reload, crash or OOM around the drops. The existing `Connection: close` only covers the client→wrangler hop, and the helper retried only GET and HEAD.

**Change** (e2e helper only, no product code):
- Replay any method **once**, only when the response matches Miniflare's exact ProxyWorker `Network connection lost` stack (local target, 500, `text/plain`, non-JSON).
- Every other mutation failure stays fail-closed. That includes workerd's plain `Internal Server Error`, a bare "Network connection lost" text, and JSON problems.
- If a replayed mutation had actually applied, it surfaces as a conflict or duplicate, not a false pass. Each replay is logged with `workers-sdk#14641`.
- GET/HEAD recycle retries are unchanged.

The owner chose this over rewriting the harness to bypass the ProxyWorker (`unstable_startWorker` plus direct UserWorker dispatch). That rewrite is the proper removal of the race and is tracked as a follow-up.

**Tests:** `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/e2e-api-client-retry.test.ts`, 11/11 passing locally. It covers a single replay for POST, PATCH and DELETE on the exact drop, at most one replay, and no replay for the other failures. biome check is clean. Repeated dispatched Cloud Tests runs are the e2e verification.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)30:T36c3,# Relates to

```
error: No matching export in "../app/src/security/remote-control-crypto.ts" for import "sealRemoteControlMessage"
    at packages/app/platforms/electrobun/src/remote-target-runner.ts:24:2
error: script "build:mobile" exited with code 1
```

Regression from #32865 (`d81fed2dbd`, merged 2026-09-28). It repointed two compatibility re-exports from `@elizaos/remote-control-host/*` to `@elizaos/plugin-browser/remote-control/*`:

- `packages/app/src/security/remote-control-crypto.ts` → `@elizaos/plugin-browser/remote-control/crypto`
- `packages/ui/src/api/direct-cloud-endpoints.ts` → `@elizaos/plugin-browser/remote-control/cloud-endpoints`

`packages/agent/scripts/build-mobile-bundle.ts` aliases `@elizaos/plugin-browser` **and every subpath** to `plugins/plugin-browser/src/mobile.ts` (longest-prefix match, `startsWith(`${name}/`)`). Both subpaths therefore resolved to the browser stub, which exports none of the crypto or cloud-endpoint symbols.

The mobile graph needs them. `packages/app/src/mobile-agent-entry.ts` registers `@elizaos/app/mobile-remote-target` (also `aospCore` in `packages/agent/src/runtime/core-plugins.ts:126`). That plugin imports the electrobun remote-target executor/rpc → `remote-target-runner.ts` → `remote-control-crypto`, and `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts` imports `direct-cloud-endpoints`.

No CI job runs `build:mobile` except the manual `android-build.yml`, which has no runs, so the break is invisible to develop CI.

Once `build:mobile` builds again, `build:android:cloud(:debug)` still fails its own Play audits on develop. These fail for the same reason: nothing runs this path in CI.

1. **Pre-gradle audit**: `capacitor.plugins.json packages differ from the Play allowlist`. `@elizaos/capacitor-network-policy`, added by `2e894ceebe` for on-device model downloads, is neither allowlisted nor stripped.
2. **Artifact audit** (`ANDROID_PLAY_MANIFEST_ALLOWLIST_FAILED`): the password-manager bridge added in `04c5080663` leaks into the Play manifest. That means `PasswordsActivity`, `BrowserNativeMessagingService` and its `org.chromium.chrome...NATIVE_MESSAGING` action, `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT`, and `<queries>` packages `com.onepassword.android`, `com.x8bit.bitwarden` and `org.chromium.chrome`.

# Change

`build-mobile-bundle.ts` resolves the six platform-neutral remote-control protocol modules to source: `crypto`, `controller`, `runtime-store`, `secure-store-contract`, `cloud-client`, `cloud-endpoints`.

- Their imports are only `node:crypto`, `@elizaos/core`, `@elizaos/core/contracts/remote-control` and `@elizaos/plugin-elizacloud/cloud-config/domain-contract`. I checked each file's imports.
- The longer alias wins over the `@elizaos/plugin-browser` stub.
- `remote-control/browser-controller` stays stubbed: it imports `../browser-service` and `../targets/remote-device-target`.
- The plugin root and all other subpaths are unchanged.

**Android Play cloud policy** (`packages/app/scripts/mobile/android/cloud-policy.ts`, `strip.ts`):

- `@elizaos/capacitor-network-policy` joins `ANDROID_CLOUD_STRIPPED_NATIVE_PLUGINS`.
  - It serves on-device model downloads: only `ElizaAgentService` and `ElizaBionicInferenceServer` use it natively, and both are already stripped.
  - The JS side is a bare side-effect import in `native-plugin-entrypoints.ts`, next to other stripped plugins (camera, canvas, appblocker, bun-runtime).
- `PasswordsActivity` and `BrowserNativeMessagingService` join `ANDROID_CLOUD_STRIPPED_COMPONENTS`, and their Java sources join `ANDROID_CLOUD_STRIPPED_JAVA_FILES`.
  - `CredentialManagerPlugin.java` is stripped too. Its only behavior is starting `PasswordsActivity`, and nothing in the cloud tree registers it.
- `ANDROID_CLOUD_STRIPPED_QUERY_PACKAGES` (Chromium placeholder, Bitwarden, 1Password, healthdata) replaces the one-off healthdata `<queries>` regex in `strip.ts`.
- `ANDROID_CLOUD_MANIFEST_MERGER_REMOVED_QUERY_PACKAGES` adds a `tools:node="remove"` marker for `${elizaChromiumPackageName}`, because the kept `plugin-native-browser-surface` library re-adds it through manifest merge (same pattern as `ANDROID_CLOUD_MANIFEST_MERGER_REMOVED_PERMISSIONS`).
- **Bug fix:** `ANDROID_CLOUD_STRIPPED_PERMISSIONS` listed `"android.permission.USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT"`, while `removeAndroidPermissionRequests` prepends `android.permission.`. The entry never matched, so the permission always leaked. It is now `"USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT"`.
- The Play allowlists themselves are unchanged (the manifest `queryPackages` allowlist is still `[]`).
  - Everything above makes the cloud build meet the existing policy.
  - Allowing the password-manager surface in the Play client would be a separate product/policy decision.

For the mobile bundle fix, I looked at two alternatives:
- **Un-stubbing plugin-browser subpaths wholesale** would pull desktop-only modules into the phone bundle.
- **Aliasing only `crypto`** fixed the first 6 errors and exposed the same failure for `cloud-endpoints` (`DEFAULT_DIRECT_CLOUD_BASE_URL`, `stripTrailingSlashes`, … imported by `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts:79-84`). So the fix covers the protocol-module class.

32:T80f,Adds `@unbrowse/plugin-unbrowse` to the community registry. The entry is `packages/registry/entries/third-party/unbrowse__plugin-unbrowse.json`, and `generated-registry.json` was regenerated with `bun run --cwd packages/registry generate`. The only change is 46 added lines for this entry.

**What it is.** An elizaOS plugin for [Unbrowse](https://unbrowse.ai). It turns websites into callable APIs through the hosted Unbrowse MCP.
- `WEB_FETCH` reads a page as clean markdown. The JS render falls back to plain HTTP.
- `UNBROWSE_DISCOVER` finds a learned API for a site.
- `UNBROWSE_RUN` runs a task on it.
- `UNBROWSE_RESUME` answers `input_required` on the same run.
- `UNBROWSE_BROWSE` opens the cloud browser, with saved logins filled from Unbrowse's vault.
- An `UNBROWSE` provider reports connection state and usage rules.

It is meant to replace `plugin-browser` for web reads and site tasks. The README documents the load order: action names are first-wins.

**Source.** [unbrowse-ai/unbrowse/plugins/elizaos](https://github.com/unbrowse-ai/unbrowse/tree/main/plugins/elizaos), MIT. `@elizaos/core` is a peer dependency, and there are no runtime dependencies because the SDK is bundled. The API key is read through `runtime.getSetting` and is never echoed; the tests assert that.

**Tested.**
- 46 offline tests, including a real `@elizaos/core` 1.7.2 `AgentRuntime` for action registration, first-wins behaviour and similes routing.
- Live tests against the hosted service: page read, a Hacker News top-stories run, and discover.
- Typecheck and build are clean. CI runs on every push in the source repo.

**Checklist**
- [x] `bun run --cwd packages/registry validate` passes (50 third-party entries).
- [x] Not in the reserved `@elizaos/*` scope.
- [x] Public repository, MIT.
- [x] `keywords` include `elizaos`.
- [ ] Published to npm: `@unbrowse/plugin-unbrowse@12.1.0` is going out from the source repo's release workflow and is not live yet. Please hold the merge until `npm view @unbrowse/plugin-unbrowse` resolves. I'll comment here when it does.33:T5b7,Fixes the last red lane on develop: `canonical / Zero-Key scenario runner` fails on `convo.context-hygiene` with "evaluator advertised context restoration with nothing deferred". Seen on 729ce4151b (run 36377571213) and reproduced locally on 6a36364ee9.

**Why.** #32870 added this scenario when the evaluator template interpolated only the applicable decisions. #32835 then deliberately changed that design for prompt caching (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/prompts/evaluator.ts`, `evaluatorContextRouteNames` doc comment):
- the reusable protocol text and response schema keep the full decision superset, so the prefix stays stable;
- eligibility moves into the per-call `# Current decision state`: `Available restoration routes: …` plus the restoration rule, only when applicable;
- `enforceEvaluatorDecisionState` rejects unavailable restoration after decoding.

The scenario still banned `RESTORE_*` anywhere in the evaluator text and schema enum, so it could never pass.

**Change.** The scenario now checks the decision state. With nothing deferred it must show `Available restoration routes: none` and carry no restoration guidance. The schema must still offer `FINISH`. No product code changes.

**Verified locally on develop 6a36364ee9:**
- `convo.context-hygiene` passes.
- Full `bun run --cwd packages/testing test:pr:e2e` passes: 27/27, 9/9, 14/14 and 34/34.
- biome check is clean.

3e:T924,Optimize the context sent to the model so action/tool search works efficiently and the agent gets only what it needs to complete the task.

## Detailed Description

**Estimate:** 5 working days. **After:** https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/31014

Use the existing context editor and trajectory viewer.

- Review representative runs to identify unnecessary instructions, duplicate context, irrelevant provider output and oversized tool descriptions/results.
- Improve context selection and composition: include what the current task needs and retrieve additional context when needed. Remove redundancy at its source rather than silently cutting off payloads.
- Verify action/tool search finds the right capabilities with concise queries, useful rankings and minimal repeated searches. Fix missed tools, irrelevant matches and unnecessary tool loading.
- Use the existing edit, rerun and compare loop to measure input tokens, retrieval overhead, latency, tool choices and task success. Record the context changes and model/settings used.
- Check both simple and multi-step tasks. Reduce context without losing required facts, tool arguments or reliable task completion.

- Correct Cloud usage attribution before comparing runs: map native `promptTokens`/`completionTokens` to the usage-event `inputTokens`/`outputTokens` contract in buffered, SSE and non-SSE fallback paths. Check current behavior and reuse applicable #27744 work.
- Verify emitted input/output/total counts match gateway usage, including a final SSE usage-only frame. Verify downstream cost estimates use the real counts when gateway cost is absent; retain unknown usage as unavailable. This fixes telemetry, not server-side credit debits.

Cloud token counts #27732 is consolidated here within the same five-day allocation. Original reproduction and consumer details remain linked in that issue.

**Done when:** before/after trajectories show lower unnecessary context and efficient action/tool search, with the agreed tasks still completing correctly. Record token and latency changes, failures and any tradeoffs. Missing usage stays unknown; estimates are labeled.

Reruns, comparison, validation and targeted fixes are included in the five days. This ticket does not build another context editor.

42:T878,## Reproduction

On current `develop` (`0831fda0f0`), the finalized-meeting event first calls `projectFinalizedTranscriptCommitments` and then runs the zone-aware meeting-ghost consumer. The commitment projector still resolves `tomorrow` and weekdays with `getUTCDay` / `setUTCDate` and fixes the resulting deadline at 17:00 UTC.

Using the production `commitmentRecordsFromTranscript` path with a meeting that starts Monday 2026-09-14 at 17:30 America/Los_Angeles and an owner segment saying `I will send the report tomorrow.` produces:

```json
{"observedAt":"2026-09-15T00:30:00.000Z","dueAt":"2026-09-16T17:00:00.000Z","sourceKey":"transcript-local-day:seg-1"}
```

The owner's intended deadline is Tuesday 2026-09-15 at 17:00 Pacific, `2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z`. The current row is Wednesday in the owner's local calendar, one day late, and disagrees with the meeting-ghost row emitted by the same finalized event.

## Impact

Owner-spoken promises from evening meetings can be ranked, displayed, and scheduled against the wrong day. The same event can persist two transcript commitment rows with conflicting deadlines.

## Scope

- Resolve the owner's effective time zone at the transcript instant in the runtime projection.
- Pass that zone into the deterministic extractor.
- Use the existing zoned-date helpers for explicit dates, `tomorrow`, weekdays, and the 17:00 wall-time deadline.
- Preserve the existing UTC behavior for callers that do not provide a zone.

## Acceptance proof

- Re-run the exact production projector reproduction and observe `2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z`.
- Exercise the real PGlite runtime projection and inspect the persisted ledger row.
- Verify the affected package tests, typecheck, lint, and root `bun run verify` on the final rebased head.

43:T145c,## Decision requested

Ratify what a Stripe refund or chargeback does to Cloud rights and actively billed resources, and when those rights may be restored.

### A. Immediate billing hold — recommended

Keep the existing idempotent proportional credit clawback, and place the organization in an explicit server-owned billing-hold state when the reversal makes its funded position invalid. New money spend and provisioning fail closed. Product must define below whether existing billable resources stop immediately or follow an explicit bounded window, and which read/export capabilities remain available. Restoration requires authoritative provider settlement plus a valid funded state; the client never infers it.

### B. Balance-only enforcement

Keep only the current credit clawback/shortfall record. The implementation floors the organization balance at zero, records any unrecovered amount, and disables auto-top-up, but there is no explicit cross-surface hold contract and already-running resources may continue accruing cost. This is simpler but leaves inconsistent behavior and unowned debt exposure across products.

### C. Manual review before restriction

Record the reversal and wait for an operator decision before changing entitlements. This reduces accidental suspension but leaves a known abuse window and requires an operator SLA, queue, audit trail, and safe default when the SLA expires.

## Exact policy fields required

Please select A, B, or C and define, without implementation guesses:

1. Trigger semantics for voluntary refunds, partial refunds, `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`, and `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`.
2. Credit treatment: proportional clawback is currently implemented; the current schema forbids negative balances and records the unrecovered portion. Confirm whether that shortfall becomes authoritative debt, is written off, or triggers another state.
3. Immediate gates for inference, storage writes/reads, domain and other one-off purchases, API-key use, new apps, containers, shared agents, and dedicated agents.
4. Treatment and exact timing for already-running billable resources (continue, suspend, stop, or delete). If timing is not immediate, provide the exact duration and who pays during it.
5. Which non-billable capabilities remain available (sign-in, billing history/receipts, data read/export, cancellation/deletion).
6. Restoration rules after a won dispute or repayment, including whether restoration is automatic or reviewed.
7. UI state, notification, receipt, support/escalation, and audit requirements.
8. Interaction with future subscription states and dunning if #20328 proceeds with subscriptions.

## Current-state evidence

Revalidated on `develop` on 2026-08-22:

- `packages/cloud/api/src/queue/stripe-event.ts` handles `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`, and `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/credits.ts` applies idempotent cumulative-delta clawbacks, floors `credit_balance` at zero under the organization lock, records `unrecovered_clawback_usd`, and disables auto-top-up.
- `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated` restores only the clawback amount that was actually applied; it does not resolve the unrecovered shortfall or establish a global restoration/hold authority.
- The merged payment-request receipt work provides provider-neutral settlement evidence, but it does not define a product-wide entitlement/resource hold.
- No open issue currently records the required cross-surface policy. Historical #10920/#10938 closed the missing Stripe clawback implementation, not this product decision.

## Scope and non-scope

This issue is a human product-decision record. It performs no payment, refund, provider call, deployment, database change, resource suspension, or production mutation. Do not implement a grace period, trial, plan entitlement, or deletion policy until the owner supplies it explicitly.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A product owner records exactly `Decision: A`, `Decision: B`, or `Decision: C`.
- [ ] Every policy field above is answered or explicitly delegated to a linked product specification.
- [ ] Bounded implementation issues cover the server authority, per-route enforcement, active-resource reconciliation, UI/receipts, and adversarial tests.
- [ ] Tests include partial refund, refund after consumption, duplicate/replayed events, lost response, dispute withdrawn/reinstated, and concurrent spend versus hold activation.

## Claim / status

Human product decision required. Keep this issue human-gated until the policy is explicit; engineering claims begin only on the bounded implementation follow-ups.

44:T4a7,## Follow-up

Nubs will look into this later. This is a deferred optimization investigation, not a confirmed correctness bug or an approved implementation change.

A combined Notes and Calendar read used four foreground model calls: response handler, planner, evaluator after Notes, and the same evaluator again after Calendar. The first evaluation decided whether to continue; the second checked completion and produced the answer.

Investigate whether demonstrably independent, read-only actions can complete before a single evaluation, without weakening conditional execution, permissions, failure handling, evidence checks, or final-answer validation. Do not simply remove the intermediate evaluation: dependent requests may need it to decide whether the next action should run.

Acceptance for any future proposal:
- Explicitly distinguish independent work from conditional or dependent work.
- Preserve cases where the second read must not execute.
- Compare actual trajectories, correctness, call counts, token usage, and latency.
- Retain the existing behavior unless a simpler safe alternative is supported by evidence.

45:T318b,Re-lands manifest-v3 restore and finishes the restore coordinator, so a user restore of a catalogued manifest-v3 backup now runs end to end in production code: quarantined create → materialize → commit → boot → attest → probe → route CAS → finalize.

Refs #20726

## #20732: restore coordinator

Fixes #20732. Everything is in code and tested against real PGlite. The only thing left is the staging drill listed below.

### Re-land (commit 1)
- Cherry-picks `9ab8150880c`, which ports closed #30820 and standujar's `feat/20732-quarantine-runtime-bridge` onto current `develop`. standujar keeps `Co-authored-by` credit.
- It applied cleanly on top of #32876, the #31334 local-state upgrade guard; the two touch different files.
- `packages/agent/FOOTPRINT-REVIEW.md` now records that the owner reversed the removal of the restore-v3 candidate modules for #20732.

### Phases
The existing phase names map onto the requested ones:
- `restoring` means materialized.
- `committed` means the generation is committed.
- `restart_attested` means booted and attested.
- `probed` means probes passed.
- `published` means routed.

| From → to | What the coordinator does |
| --- | --- |
| `reserved`/`vault_seeded` → `container_created` | Runs the existing exact quarantined create. An ambiguous provider outcome goes through the existing claim-fenced reconciliation. |
| `container_created` → `restoring` | 1. Starts the quarantine host.<br>2. Prepares private roots inside the container (new controller worker). Their device/inode identities are recorded.<br>3. Streams the catalogue into the quarantine through the guarded materializer journal, using the worker's KMS key bundle and the storage registry. Each object uses its own persisted endpoint.<br>4. Records the sealed candidate receipt and session. |
| `restoring` → `committed` | Prepares and commits the generation inside the still-networkless container, then records the committed-generation handoff. |
| `committed` → `restart_attested` | 1. Snapshots the previous route and observes the previous runtime.<br>2. Stops the previous runtime **in place** (it is retained, so it can be restarted).<br>3. Serving transition: detaches the exact container from `none`, attaches the agent network (which activates host ports reserved at create) and restarts the quarantine PID 1.<br>4. Hands over a boot grant.<br>5. Launches the committed generation.<br>6. Polls a private-socket probe until it returns an HMAC-signed boot attestation under the per-restore activation token.<br>7. Sandbox activation moves `restore_pending` → `restart_attested` and the operation advances, in one transaction. |
| `restart_attested` → `probed` | Runs three fresh-nonce signed probes. Each checks the MAC, the identities, the committed-generation digest and the listen port. |
| `probed` → `published` | Route CAS, in one transaction:<br>- proves the canonical route still equals the snapshot;<br>- adopts the provider-settled replacement (`lifecycle_committed`);<br>- activates the generation (`active`, published/dispatched/completed);<br>- points `sandbox_id`/`node_id`/`container_name`/`bridge_url`/`health_url`/`image_digest` at the restored container. |
| `published` → `finalized` | 1. Appends the immutable activation publication from the active generation.<br>2. Removes the previous container, bound to its identity, and releases its node slot once.<br>3. Writes the final restore receipt (`commitAgentBackupRestore`, backup → `restore_verified`).<br>4. Finalizes the operation with the receipt and releases the lease. |

### Failure policy (fail closed at each phase)
- **Before the previous runtime is touched:** failures retry with exponential backoff up to `AGENT_BACKUP_RESTORE_MAX_ATTEMPTS`. After that the restore activation is blocked and the operation fails terminally. The route never moves.
- **After the previous runtime is stopped** (`committed`, `restart_attested`, `probed`): any failure rolls back. The restored container is stopped, the retained previous runtime is restarted, the activation is blocked, the operation fails terminally and the lease is released. If rollback is incomplete, the operation is recorded as `AGENT_BACKUP_RESTORE_ROLLBACK_INCOMPLETE` and the error is rethrown so an operator is alerted.
- **Cleanup after a terminal failure:** the failed container is fenced (`cleanup_in_progress`) before it is removed exactly. It is then settled as `cleanup_proven`, and its node slot is released once. Settling frees the agent's replacement fence, so later restores and upgrades are not blocked. A cycle sweep retries any interrupted cleanup. A later restore may replace the `blocked` activation that the failed restore left behind.
- **After the route CAS:** rollback no longer applies. Finalization retries until it settles.
- **Lapsed lease:** an operation whose lease lapsed with no live claim is closed explicitly as `AGENT_BACKUP_RESTORE_LEASE_EXPIRED`.
- **Evidence is write-once:** every piece of phase evidence lives in `agent_backup_restore_operations.serving_state` (migration `0495`, journal updated). A lost response therefore replays and never re-executes.

### Production caller
- `POST /api/v1/eliza/agents/:agentId/restore` → `elizaSandboxService.restore()` → `admitCoordinatedAgentBackupRestore`.
- When `AGENT_BACKUP_RESTORE_COORDINATOR_ENABLED=1` and the selected backup is a catalogued manifest-v3 backup in a restorable state, the route acquires the restore lease for `AGENT_BACKUP_RESTORE_WORKER_ID`, opens the operation and returns **202** with the operation id.
- A second request reports the in-progress restore. Another coordinator's restore returns 409.
- Legacy snapshot rows keep the existing push/re-provision path.
- The coordinator runs as a `restore-coordinator` stage of the existing backup-catalogue worker daemon. That stage shares the worker's KMS key bundle and storage registry.

### Deliberate change to the quarantine create
- Docker cannot add port bindings to an existing container. The exact quarantine is therefore created with its two host ports reserved (`-p`), still attached only to `none`, where the bindings are inert.
- The create readback and every command fingerprint now require the reserved bindings.
- The only transition that attaches a network is the coordinator's serving transition, after the committed generation is proven.

### Agent side
- `agent-backup-restore-v3-controller-worker`: one-shot, private, with three methods: `prepareRoots`, `commitGeneration` and `writeBootGrant`.
- `agent-backup-restore-v3-restored-runtime`: boots `startEliza({ restoredGeneration })` behind an exclusive lock and serves signed attestations on a 0600 unix socket.
- `agent-backup-restore-v3-probe-client`: relays one probe.
- Contract: `@elizaos/core/contracts/agent-backup-restore-v3-serving`.
- Agent restore tests had wall-clock test budgets that timed out under load. Those budgets are now load-tolerant. Assertions and deliberately short expiry deadlines are unchanged.

### Remaining: staging drill (needs an authorized staging run)
1. On a real Hetzner node, confirm Docker accepts `-p` with `--network none` at create. Then confirm that `docker network disconnect none` followed by `docker network connect <agent-net>` on the stopped exact container activates the reserved bindings.
2. Confirm the built image ships the three new `dist/services/*.js` entrypoints and the new core contract in `packages/core/dist`. Confirm the runtime launched with `docker exec -d` (using the image's `/opt/tsx` loader) inherits the agent env and binds `ELIZA_PORT`.
3. Restore a real agent and verify functional LLM recall of pre-backup memory through the published `bridge_url`.
4. Exercise a forced probe failure and confirm the previous runtime restarts.

## #24407: fair admission and legacy cron

Refs #24407. Verified; the cron is **not** retired.
- The minute admission (`/api/v1/cron/agent-backup-admission`) is fair: 64 sharded durable cursors, bounded per-invocation claim and enrollment budgets, and explicit deferral.
- It only enrolls agents whose activation is `active` with published activation authority. Before this PR nothing in production wrote that state. After it, only coordinator restores do.
- Retiring the six-hour `enqueueScheduledBackups` caller would therefore leave every legacy agent with no scheduled backup.
- The cron comment now records this. Retirement needs an activation backfill for legacy agents first; the admission route stays gated off (`AGENT_BACKUP_ADMISSION_CALLER_ENABLED=0`).

## #23235: capture escalation and stranded-row plan

Refs #23235.
- A capture operation that keeps failing retryably is escalated once it reaches `AGENT_BACKUP_CAPTURE_ESCALATION_ATTEMPTS` claims (default 8). The typical case is the available-memory gate.
- The escalated failure becomes a trusted **terminal** disposition, `AGENT_BACKUP_V2_CAPTURE_ESCALATED`. It carries the exact spool-cleanup authority, so it settles through the vetted terminal path.
- The worker raises `BACKUP_CAPTURE_V2_ESCALATED` and logs the org and backup.
- Stale-runtime-authority failures are never escalated.

### Plan for repairing stranded rows (not executed; live customer rows)
1. **Inventory (read-only).** Collect every `agent_sandboxes` row in `deletion_pending`/`deletion_failed` older than 24h. For each, record its last error, `deletion_attempt_id`, `bridge_url`, node and whether a container is present (observe only).
2. **Classify.**
   - (a) node-unreachable, where the node is now reachable;
   - (b) permanently failed on a since-fixed defect (#23117 `toISOString`, job timeout);
   - (c) capture refused, where the customer wants the state gone;
   - (d) the container is already absent.
3. **Repair per class, through existing lifecycle authority only (no raw SQL).**
   - (a)/(b): re-enqueue deletion with `reEnqueueFailedDeletions`.
   - (d): run the reap path, which releases the allocation.
   - (c): run `shutdown` with `stateLossAcknowledged` under an operator-recorded authorization.
4. **`bridge_url` after reap.** Clear it in `releaseDeletionAllocationOnReap`, so a later attempt does not refuse on a dead bridge. This is a small follow-up code change with a PGlite regression test.
5. **Dry run, then execute.** Dry-run in staging, then run in production with an operator present. Take a before/after row diff as the audit record.

Still open under #23235: the customer-reachable acknowledged-state-loss delete path.

46:T16bf,# Relates to

Action tasks loaded unrelated schemas, sometimes finished without requested readback, and rebuilt avoidable prompt context. Simple arithmetic could unnecessarily enter evaluation. Paused work could lose its original domain when the response handler returned a simple reply.

# Change

Keep the response handler focused on contexts and intents. Use task-scoped `DISCOVER_ACTIONS`, operation-specific schemas, canonical tool ordering, stable evaluator instructions and instruction-aware cache affinity. Document verification/reference codes no longer infer programming work. Complete authorized source text and receipts remain intact.

Continue against every requested outcome, with prerequisite barriers, verified receipts, explicit additional-budget resume, fresh authorization and cancellation. Saved schemas are not reused. Ambiguous effects are parked rather than replayed. Preserve external continuation-worker fixes. Completed simple answers stay on the one-call path.

Preserve exact FILE bytes and final-line-ending evidence. A scoped native object envelope addresses observed Cerebras Qwen argument boundary loss while retaining native schemas, validation, cancellation and canonical hooks. Other providers and handler/evaluator transport are unchanged. Distinguish document identifiers from integrity metadata in evaluation.

# Testing

## #22961 / #23022: creator monetization retired (affiliates stay live)

Fixes #22961
Fixes #23022

**Accrual stopped. History kept.**
- MCP calls (`recordUsage`, `recordUsageWithoutDeduction`) no longer write either creator leg: no creator org credit and no `mcp` redeemable earning. New `mcp_usage` rows record `creator_earnings = 0`. The base price and platform fee go to `platform_earnings`, and the buyer charge is unchanged.
- Agent inference markup and app creator monetization are switched off by 0500. Turning either back on returns HTTP 410 with `code: creator_monetization_retired`; this is a typed `CreatorMonetizationRetiredError` that extends `ApiError`.
- Earnings-funded hosting (`pay_as_you_go_from_earnings`) was an automatic payout of the frozen balance. 0500 turns it off and sets the column default to false. The settings API refuses to turn it back on, and an account-deletion restore no longer re-enables it.
- No ledger rows are deleted or rewritten.

**Creator payout entry points.** Each of these now returns a typed 410:
- `POST /api/v1/redemptions` and `GET /api/v1/redemptions/quote`
- admin approve in `POST /api/admin/redemptions` (admin reject still works; it returns the pending lock to the frozen balance)
- `POST /api/v1/apps/:id/earnings/withdraw`
- `POST /api/cron/process-redemptions`, which is also removed from the cron schedule

The on-chain payout processor and the `lockForRedemption` / `refundRedemption` / `convertToCredits` helpers are deleted. Redemption history and the balance read (now always `canRedeem: false`) stay.

**Affiliate payouts stay live.** The affiliate program continues; only creator, MCP and app monetization is retired.
- `POST /api/v1/earnings/payout/stripe-connect/onboard` is restored for users who have an affiliate code. Anyone else gets 403.
- `POST /api/v1/earnings/payout/stripe-connect/transfer` is restored. It is still admin-gated and still a compensating saga. It now debits through `affiliate-payouts.ts`, and a definitive Stripe rejection re-credits the amount as an affiliate earning. No cron is needed, because an operator triggers each transfer.
- **The gate.** A payout may debit at most `min(affiliate net since retirement, available balance − frozen available)`. "Affiliate net since retirement" is the sum of the user's `affiliate` ledger earnings and adjustments after the retirement instant: the user's statement `frozen_at`, or the new `creator_monetization_retirement.retired_at` singleton written by 0500.
  - The check and the debit run in one transaction under the same per-user advisory lock as the ledger, so concurrent payouts cannot both pass.
  - A same-key retry reuses the prior debit.
  - Anything more returns 409 `affiliate_payout_exceeds_payable`.
  - Non-affiliate earnings after retirement (revenue shares and similar) are not payable through this path.
- **Not payable here:** affiliate earnings that were already unpaid at retirement. They are inside the frozen statement and are settled manually with it.
- The statement shows `affiliatePayableUsd`, and the Earnings tab shows it as "Affiliate earnings available for payout".

**Freeze and statement.**
- 0500 copies every user's unpaid balance (`available_balance + total_pending`) into `creator_earnings_retirement_statements`, broken down by source. The table has no foreign keys, so the record survives account deletion. The copy is idempotent.
- `GET /api/v1/earnings/statement` returns the read-only statement.
- The Monetization "Earnings" tab now shows this statement. The redemption UI, the app earnings dashboard, the withdraw dialog and the app monetization settings are removed.

### Follow-up for manual handling: affected orgs and amounts

No local database was reachable (`DATABASE_URL` unset, nothing on localhost:5432), so this lists no orgs or amounts; it gives the queries to run instead. Run them against production **after 0500 is applied**:

```sql
-- Frozen creator balances per organization (what is owed)
SELECT s.organization_id, o.name AS organization_name,
       count(*) AS users, sum(s.unpaid_balance_usd) AS unpaid_usd,
       sum(s.available_balance_usd) AS available_usd,
       sum(s.pending_redemption_usd) AS pending_redemption_usd,
       sum(s.earned_from_mcps_usd) AS from_mcps_usd,
       sum(s.earned_from_agents_usd) AS from_agents_usd,
       sum(s.earned_from_apps_usd) AS from_apps_usd,
       sum(s.earned_from_affiliates_usd) AS from_affiliates_usd,
       sum(s.earned_from_revenue_shares_usd) AS from_revenue_shares_usd
FROM creator_earnings_retirement_statements s
LEFT JOIN organizations o ON o.id = s.organization_id
WHERE s.status = 'frozen'
GROUP BY s.organization_id, o.name
ORDER BY unpaid_usd DESC;

-- In-flight redemptions an operator must reconcile or reject (no cron pays them now)
SELECT id, user_id, status, usd_value, network, broadcast_tx_hash, created_at
FROM token_redemptions
WHERE status IN ('pending', 'approved', 'processing')
ORDER BY created_at;

-- Mark one balance settled after paying it out of band
UPDATE creator_earnings_retirement_statements
SET status = 'settled_manually', settled_at = now(), settlement_reference = '<ticket or transfer id>'
WHERE user_id = '<user id>' AND status = 'frozen';
```

```sql
-- Affiliate earnings payable through Stripe Connect now, per user
WITH cutoff AS (
  SELECT e.user_id,
         COALESCE(s.frozen_at, (SELECT retired_at FROM creator_monetization_retirement)) AS since,
         CASE WHEN s.status = 'frozen' THEN s.available_balance_usd ELSE 0 END AS frozen_available,
         e.available_balance
  FROM redeemable_earnings e
  LEFT JOIN creator_earnings_retirement_statements s ON s.user_id = e.user_id
)
SELECT c.user_id,
       GREATEST(0, LEAST(COALESCE(SUM(l.amount), 0), c.available_balance - c.frozen_available)) AS payable_usd
FROM cutoff c
LEFT JOIN redeemable_earnings_ledger l
  ON l.user_id = c.user_id AND l.earnings_source = 'affiliate'
 AND l.entry_type IN ('earning', 'adjustment') AND l.created_at > c.since
GROUP BY c.user_id, c.available_balance, c.frozen_available
HAVING GREATEST(0, LEAST(COALESCE(SUM(l.amount), 0), c.available_balance - c.frozen_available)) > 0;
```

### Paid MCP listings retired

With creator monetization retired, a listing price would bill buyers for revenue that reaches no creator.
- **Existing listings.** Migration **0502** makes every listing free: `pricing_type = 'free'`, both prices set to 0, x402 off. The prior pricing is saved in `metadata.retired_paid_listing` for audit. Usage history is untouched, and the column defaults are now free.
- **New prices refused.** `POST /api/v1/mcps` and `PUT /api/v1/mcps/:id` refuse any price with 410 `creator_monetization_retired` (capability `paid_mcp_listing`). Setting a listing to free is still allowed.
- **Proxy.** It never charges a listing price, even for a stale row. A free call skips the credit reservation, since zero-cost reservations are rejected. It runs the deferred strong credential check before calling upstream, then records usage at zero.
- **Editor.** The MCP editor no longer shows pricing or x402 controls. It explains that listings are free and always submits `pricingType: "free"`.
- **Not unlisted.** No listing was unlisted, because none has pricing that is intrinsic to the listing. x402 listings are made free too.

Listings affected by 0502. There is no local database, so run this before applying 0502, or use the `metadata` check after it:

```sql
-- Before 0502
SELECT id, organization_id, slug, name, status, is_public, pricing_type,
       credits_per_request, x402_price_usd, x402_enabled, total_requests
FROM user_mcps
WHERE pricing_type <> 'free' OR COALESCE(credits_per_request, 0) <> 0
   OR COALESCE(x402_price_usd, 0) <> 0 OR x402_enabled = true
ORDER BY total_requests DESC;

-- After 0502
SELECT id, organization_id, slug, name, status, metadata -> 'retired_paid_listing' AS previous_pricing
FROM user_mcps
WHERE metadata ? 'retired_paid_listing';
```

## #23020: affiliate surcharge disclosure

Fixes #23020

- **One formula.** `auto-top-up-charge-breakdown.ts` holds the formula shared by the durable charge snapshot and a new server preview. The formula, percentages, rounding and activation gate are unchanged.
- **Billing API.** `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/billing/settings` return `autoTopUp.chargePreview`: the credited base, affiliate markup, platform fee, total card charge, and whether attribution is `none`, `affiliate` or `unavailable`. `?previewAmount=` quotes an amount that has not been saved yet.
- **Billing UI.** The auto top-up card shows those lines as separate rows before the customer saves, and re-quotes on the server when the amount is edited. It never computes money in the client. When attribution is unavailable, it says no surcharge will be charged.
- **Receipts.** Invoices store `charge_breakdown`, parsed from the signed durable metadata. `GET /api/invoices/:id` and the ledger entries (`chargeBreakdown`) expose it, and the invoice page shows the markup, platform fee and total on separate lines. A breakdown whose lines do not add up to the charged total is never shown.
- **Other paths.** One-time checkout applies no affiliate surcharge, so it has nothing to disclose. MCP calls already recorded `base_amount_usd`, `affiliate_fee_usd` and `platform_fee_usd` on their credit transactions and usage rows.
- **Copy.** New UI copy is in `en.json`. The affiliate pricing example no longer promises a "redeemable token balance".

## #22967: frozen/sleeping retention

Fixes #22967

This builds on existing machinery rather than adding a parallel path: the billing-stop authority, the automatic resume from #30702, the Dedicated fallback `retain_until` from #32856, the sleep lifecycle, the email service and the backup `billing-freeze` retention reason, which previously had no writers.

- **Clock table.** 0501 adds `agent_funding_retentions`, one open row per stopped agent.
- **Hourly cron.** `/api/cron/agent-funding-retention` does the following:
  - **Discovery.** It finds provider-confirmed `billing_request` stops that are still the agent's latest decision (credits exhausted), and open plan-withdrawal fallbacks (plan lapsed past grace). The deadline is the stop time + 30 days, or the fallback's `retain_until`.
  - **Notices.** It sends the 7-day and 1-day deletion emails through the email service, using a new template with all 8 locales. Each notice is claimed on its row before sending and released if the send fails, so it goes out once and failures retry.
  - **No deletion without notice.**
    - A clock first seen late, such as an agent already stopped when this ships, gets a deadline at least 7 days out.
    - A deadline reached without the 1-day notice sends that notice and moves the deadline one day later.
  - **Deadline.** It enqueues the existing `agent_sleep` job, which proves a durable backup before removing the container. When the agent is `sleeping`, the latest backup is pinned for 90 days: v2 rows get `retention_reason = 'billing-freeze'` and `retention_until = now + 90d`. Legacy inline rows have no time-based GC.
  - **Paying resumes.** The existing resume paths restart the same agent id. The clock closes (`funding_restored`) once the agent is running or the fallback moved to recovery. It never deletes while an `agent_resume` or `agent_wake` job is in flight.
  - **Blocked case.** An agent stopped with an unbacked in-place runtime (#30746) is reported as blocked instead of losing state.

## #31018 / #31328: dstack TEE deployment
Refs #31018, Refs #31328

This adds `packages/app/deploy/dstack-alpha/`, next to `Dockerfile.ci`, `confidential-bootstrap.mjs` and the existing sign, encrypt and provision tools it reuses.

- **Pinned image build.** `build-image.sh` builds `Dockerfile.ci` from digest-pinned node and bun bases listed in `image.lock.json`. `Dockerfile.ci` gains `NODE_IMAGE`/`BUN_IMAGE` args whose defaults are unchanged, and `verify-agent-image.sh` gains `DOCKER_BUILD_EXTRA_ARGS`. The script then overlays the digest-pinned `dstack-verifier` 0.6.0, pushes the image and records its registry digest.
- **Measured app-compose.** `scripts/alpha-dstack.ts render` writes the exact app-compose bytes. The compose pins the image by digest, keeps state on an `eliza-state` volume at `/data/eliza`, uses `restart: unless-stopped` and health-checks `/api/health`.
- **Model and ingress settings.** Text goes to Cerebras. Embeddings use Eliza Cloud or an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, and STT/TTS use Eliza Cloud. Local inference is off (plugin skipped, cloud deployment runtime, no local embeddings, no FFI llama). Ingress binds 0.0.0.0 and requires `ELIZA_API_TOKEN` (`ELIZA_REQUIRE_LOCAL_AUTH=1`). The `dstack-cpu` boot gate is on.
- **Secrets.** Secrets appear only as `allowed_envs` names. `encrypt-env` sends them through dstack's KMS-encrypted environment, and `confidential-bootstrap.mjs` authenticates them before the agent loads. No secrets are in the repo.
- **Attestation.** There is a new authenticated route, `POST /api/tee/dstack/attestation`, that returns raw guest evidence for an operator nonce. `verify-attestation` appraises it off-box with `verifyDstackAttestation` against the signed release. It also checks the signed Cerebras processor approval with `createConfidentialHostPolicy`.
- **README.** Covers deploy, verify, pairing, and upgrade/rollback.

## #31021: phone pairing contract
Refs #31021

- **core.** Adds a `remote-agent-pairing` contract (endpoints, bearer auth) and a strict QR/deep-link payload: `elizaos://remote/agent-pair?v=1&url=&code=&instance=`. The payload carries a one-time code and the issuing instance, never a token. The pair schemas gain an optional `instanceId`.
- **agent.** The agent-only server now reports a per-process `instanceId`, which the app's `pair()` needs. Before this change, pairing against a hosted agent-only server failed with `PAIRING_NOT_READY`. `pair-code` can now be read from loopback or with the API token, so a CVM operator can use it. `pair` rejects a code issued by another instance with 409.
- **app.** The compat `pair-code` route also admits an authenticated OWNER. The deep-link handler exchanges the code against a trusted origin (the build-pinned `VITE_ELIZA_REMOTE_FALLBACK_API_BASE` for Alpha APKs) before the normal connect, which asks the user to confirm.
- **ui.** `exchangeRemoteAgentPairing` checks the origin before sending any request and binds the exchange to the issuing instance.
- **Operator CLI.** `alpha-dstack.ts pair-link` prints the payload.

Verification:
- `test/remote-agent-pairing-http.test.ts` (packages/agent, real HTTP): 1 passed. It covers the wrong token, the wrong instance, a reused code, and bearer use afterwards.
- `src/platform/remote-agent-pairing.test.ts` (packages/ui): 7 passed.
