49:T2efb,Refs #22957

Eliza Cloud has two ways to pay: **Plus/Pro subscriptions** (a monthly allowance plus plan limits, from #32838/#32854) and **pay-as-you-go prepaid credits**. This PR puts the owner's decisions on the consolidated #22957 children into code, one commit per child. `packages/cloud` has no billing README, so the policy table is here.

| Policy | Decision (as implemented) |
|---|---|
| Storage prices (#22956) | `STORAGE_PRICING` in `shared/src/lib/constants/pricing.ts` is the only storage price source. PUT costs $0.0001 plus $0.000000001 per byte; GET, HEAD, list and presign cost $0.00005 each; DELETE is free. There is no per-GB fee. The `service_pricing` storage rows are a read-only copy, and the admin API refuses to edit them. |
| Top-ups and packs (#22963) | Pay-as-you-go top-ups accept any whole-cent amount from **$5 to $1,000** per checkout, on card, crypto and direct wallet. The $10/$25/$50/$100 buttons are presets, not packs. Credits always equal the amount paid, with no bonus. Fixed packs are retired, but historical pack orders and ledger rows can still be read. |
| API keys (#22958) | API keys are free. Plans cap how many a user can create: **pay-as-you-go 5, Plus 10, Pro 25**. Keys the system creates (default, explorer, sign-in, app and agent keys) don't count. A deactivated key counts until it is deleted. A key's own `rate_limit` (requests per minute across all endpoints) is a cap under the plan tier; it never raises the tier. |
| RPM tiers (#23019) | Subscribers get their plan's tier. For pay-as-you-go, the $5 and $100 thresholds count only **purchased** credits: Stripe, OxaPay crypto, x402, direct wallet (paid USD only, not the promo bonus) and payment requests. Refunds and chargebacks subtract; a won dispute adds back. Promo, signup, referral, affiliate, earnings, MCP and refund credits never count. |
| Crypto and x402 refunds (#22968) | Refunds are paid **only as Eliza Cloud credits**. Nothing is sent back on-chain or to fiat. Each refund is capped at the USD actually paid, can be replayed safely with the same refund key, and goes only to the org that owns the payment. The policy is stated at checkout and in the x402 top-up payment prompt. |
| Generated media (#20956) | Generated media counts toward the **single storage quota**; there is no separate media quota. If the quota is already full, the request is refused before any charge. If the output doesn't fit once generated, it isn't stored and the provider cost is charged. Storage is released only after the object is confirmed deleted. |

Migrations 0503–0505 are appended to the journal after develop's 0500–0502. They were first written as 0496–0498 and renumbered when #32907 merged, because the deployed migration ledger rejects entries inserted before already-applied migrations.

Fixes #22963

## #22958: API keys
Fixes #22958

- `apiKeys` is now a resource ceiling:
  - added to `FREE_RESOURCE_CEILINGS` and to the Plus/Pro catalogue entries;
  - `readOrganizationQuotaPolicy` resolves it for pay-as-you-go and for subscriber orgs;
  - a lapsed subscription falls back to 5.
- `POST /api/v1/api-keys` creates through `createUserManaged`:
  - it takes the org policy lock and counts non-deleted user-created keys;
  - at the limit it throws `ApiKeyLimitExceededError`, returned as 403 `api_key_limit_exceeded` with `used`, `limit` and `remaining`;
  - rotating a key keeps its attribution.
- `GET` returns `usage`, and the API keys page shows it.
- Migration **0505** adds `api_keys.user_created`, backfills it for existing live user keys (system-provisioned keys are excluded), and adds a partial index.
- **Per-key `rate_limit`, now enforced.** Nothing read this field before, but keys do carry custom values: the explorer key is minted at 100, app keys at 10000, and the API accepts 1–100000. So the field is kept and enforced.
  - It means requests per minute for the key across every inference endpoint, and it only narrows the plan tier.
  - `enforceOrgRateLimit` takes the presenting `apiKeyId` and reads the cap inside the locked policy transaction. Policy admission now passes its transaction to the callback.
  - The cap is applied only when it can bind, i.e. when it is below the plan's combined per-minute tier. Default 1000 keys therefore add no work.
  - The admission Durable Object checks the tier window and the per-key window in one serialized decision. Key windows are dropped after their minute ends.
  - The Redis limiter path checks an `apikey:<id>:rpm` window.
  - A key that authenticated but has since disappeared fails closed with a 503.
  - Chat completions, chat, messages, embeddings and the generative routes pass the key id.
- Tests:

Fixes #20956

Before admission it checks the org still has storage headroom; if not it returns a typed 413 `storage_quota_exceeded`. It then reserves the exact output bytes before writing to R2.

## #30806 — Worker admission (Refs #30806)
- **(a)** Affiliate-marked and flat-priced subscriber requests on the Worker now use the deferred Durable Object lease-and-settle lane.
  - The affiliate payout contract is pinned in the lease recovery context.
  - The payout is enqueued inside the funding transaction, so it commits atomically with the debit. Replays and alarm recovery never pay twice. The DO validates the pinned contract.
  - Found and fixed: the synchronous subscriber reservation never enqueued its affiliate payout. `subscriptionFundingService.settleInTransaction` ignored the pinned `affiliatePayout` metadata.
  - Non-Worker callers stay synchronous. The reason is documented in `isDeferredSubscriberLaneEligible`: they have no Worker lifetime and no DO for leases or alarm recovery.
- **(b)** New flag `INFERENCE_SNAPSHOT_ADMISSION_ENABLED`, on in staging and off in production. With it on, the warm Worker path serves admission from the published admission snapshot. No policy transaction, dispatch-policy transaction or auth-cache policy read runs before dispatch.
  - The snapshot now carries billing-hold state and subscriber funding capacity. Both are read under the publishing policy lock.
  - Every publication advances the org DO's policy generation. A lease or dispatch carrying an older generation fails closed with `409 inference_admission_policy_stale`, and the projection is repaired under `waitUntil`.
  - Aged snapshots, held orgs and subscribers without published capacity take the authoritative path.
  - Documented residual: a policy or balance write that has not yet been republished is visible to the DO only after the snapshot max age (the auth-context TTL).
- **(c)** The production strong-revocation (#30796) canary procedure is documented in `wrangler.toml`. Production is not flipped.
- Remaining: the live Worker→Cerebras trace and certification, the staging soak of snapshot admission, and the production canary.

## #29921 / #22934 — connector cutover guarantees (Refs #29921)
Decision (delegated): during cutover, ingress is held until Dedicated is attested. No message is dropped or delivered twice.
- **Cloud:** the Shared seal (423) and the committed cutover (409) now become a typed `PersonalCutoverHoldError`. Before this, they surfaced as a 500 and as a terminal conflict. The route returns them as a retryable `503 personal_cutover_in_progress` with `Retry-After`.
- **Gateway:** for acknowledged-before-processing providers (Blooio, Twilio), a held turn is persisted durably in Redis instead of being reopened and lost.
  - It is stored as a record plus a sorted-set index, and its dedup key is marked "held".
  - A leased drainer redelivers it with the original message id until Cloud accepts it, then records the delivery once.
  - The one-hour budget expires to visible failure handling.
- Remaining: the live Shared→Dedicated composed transition through a real provider.

4b:T1742,## Fixes #30825

Agents larger than the 128 MiB in-memory v1 snapshot ceiling had no way to make a local backup. This PR adds a streamed local format (v2) and a matching restore.

**Capture.** `createLocalAgentBackup` still tries v1 first. If v1 refuses with `AgentSnapshotBudgetExceededError` and the runtime uses a filesystem PGlite, it writes a v2 archive instead (`*.agent-backup-v2`).
- The PGlite data dir is copied (clone-on-write where the filesystem supports it) while the adapter holds its query and lifecycle fences. This uses a new plugin-sql method, `withPgliteDataDirQuiesced` / `withQuiescedDataDir`.
- The copied database, media, state files, vault and config are then streamed as capture-v2 frames through the existing `streamAgentBackupV2Capture`, which provides the frame hashes, component digests and digest chain.
- Memory stays bounded at one 1 MiB segment plus one frame. No archive is built in memory.
- Sizes are checked from `stat` before anything is streamed. Anything over the capture-v2 limits (1 GiB plaintext, frame and file counts) still returns the structured 413 through the same typed error, so the route and UI need no changes.

**Encryption is preserved.**
- Each backup gets its own AES-256-GCM data key, wrapped by the existing `agent-backup` KMS system key.
- The body is encrypted in 1 MiB segments. Each segment's AAD covers the header, the segment index, the final flag and the trailer, so truncating, reordering or editing the archive makes decryption fail.
- Listing reads only the plaintext header and trailer.

**Restore** (`restoreLocalAgentBackup` picks v1 or v2 from the file extension):
1. Authenticates and parses the whole archive into a temp staging dir under `backups/`, checking every frame and component digest.
2. Recreates the empty PostgreSQL layout directories, checks `PG_VERSION`, and opens the staged database with PGlite.
3. Only after all of that does it stop the runtime.
4. Swaps the PGlite dir, media and `.vault-pglite` as whole directories, and state and vault files one by one. Every rename is journaled. If any step fails, the journal is replayed in reverse and the live data is left as it was.

**Listing, pruning and retired-archive review/purge** handle both formats. Review decrypts and authenticates the whole v2 archive, and purge hashes files in streaming fashion.

### Verification
- `bunx vitest run test/local-backup-v2-restore.test.ts` (new, real HTTP host plus filesystem PGlite): 4/4 pass.
  - A small agent still gets v1.
  - A 144 MiB agent gets a v2 archive. The test checks the magic bytes, 0600 permissions, that a plaintext canary is absent, and that listing shows both formats.
  - A corrupted archive is rejected with the runtime still running and no data changed.
  - An injected swap failure (a read-only directory) rolls back the database, media and state.
  - Restore over HTTP brings back byte-identical media and state and the database rows as they were at backup time.
- `bunx vitest run test/backup-capture-http.test.ts`: 3/3 pass. The 413 case now uses a 1.5 GiB sparse file (the v2 limit), because 200 MiB is now backed up successfully.
- `plugins/plugin-sql`: `src/__tests__/pglite-adapter-liveness.test.ts` 2/2 pass.
- `tsc --noEmit` passes for both `packages/agent` and `plugins/plugin-sql`. Biome is clean on the changed files. The 36 warnings from the package-wide agent `lint:check` all come from files this PR doesn't touch.
- The repo-wide `bun run verify` was not run because the shared machine is overloaded.

### Crash recovery (second commit)
- Each restore writes a durable journal to its `backups/.restore-*` directory. The journal moves through three phases: `staging`; `swapping`, which records the full move plan before the first rename; and `committed`. Each phase is fsynced when it is written.
- An in-process swap failure now rolls back with the same journal algorithm that runs at startup.
- `startEliza` calls `recoverInterruptedLocalBackupRestores()` before it loads the config or opens PGlite:
  - a `swapping` journal is rolled back to the previous data;
  - a `committed` journal is rolled forward (only the work files remain to remove);
  - staging-only work is discarded.
  Each outcome is logged with the structured logger, and the work directory is removed afterwards.
- The crashed restore leaves its backup-authority claim behind. Recovery releases it only when that journal names the claim's operation id and the owner process no longer exists. If another live process holds the claim, recovery is skipped with a warning. A dead claim that no journal names returns the typed error `AGENT_BACKUP_RESTORE_RECOVERY_BLOCKED`.
- Two new crash-injection points drive a real child-process crash in the test: `backup-restore-swap`, which hard-exits after the database and media are swapped, and `backup-restore-commit`. After each crash the test runs recovery, the step the next startup performs. It checks that the database rows, media and state are rolled back or forward, that the claim is released, and that authority-guarded work proceeds.
- A Postgres agent over the ceiling still gets the typed 413. The error now says explicitly that v2 local backup supports PGlite only, and the body includes `streamedBackupSupported: false`.

Additional verification: `local-backup-v2-restore.test.ts` passes 5/5 (including the crash test), `backup-capture-http.test.ts` 3/3, and `connector-credential-durable.test.ts` 2/2 (a real `startElizaProcess` boot through the new startup hook). `tsc` passes and Biome is clean on the changed files.

### Remaining
- Postgres-backed agents above the in-memory ceiling get the explicit 413; v2 supports filesystem PGlite only.
- The Postgres 413 wording has no automated test, because the harness has no Postgres runtime.
- The native app still needs to be rebuilt and a backup/restore confirmed there, as the issue asks.

## #20726 — durable sandbox backup/restore (Refs #20726)

### Restore re-land dropped
The re-land of #30820 was dropped from this PR. `dce4782e17` deliberately retired the manifest-v3 candidate-filesystem substrate (see `packages/agent/FOOTPRINT-REVIEW.md`: unfinished staging machinery with no callers), and re-landing it without a production caller would reverse that decision. #20732 stays open on that basis.

### Local-state upgrade guard
`fix(cloud): refuse blue/green upgrades that would strand local agent state`

The fleet upgrade can cut a local-state Dedicated agent over to a fresh volume on another node, leaving its history behind (#31334). It now refuses before any placement or container work and returns a rollback-safe result, so the old container keeps serving.

### What this does NOT finish
- **Restore still stops before activation.** The restore operation still ends at `restore_pending`. No production dispatcher or cron calls the quarantined-create runtime, the restore coordinator, or the new materializer services.
  - **Remaining code:** advance `container_created → restoring → committed → restart_attested → probed → published`. That covers the restored boot, signed readiness/functional probe, funding/activation/route CAS, and old-target cleanup.
  - **Remaining evidence:** a separately authorized staging wipe → restore → remembered-fact drill with measured RPO/RTO.

## #29922 — exact-source staging certification (Refs #29922)

- **Release authority.** `fix(cloud): fail closed on unattested served builds in release authority` (Refs #27229, #28746)
  - The freshness guard used to skip a deploy whenever a newer commit was already being served. It now skips only when that newer commit is reachable from the canonical ref; otherwise it deploys over it.
  - The staging certificate is issued only when this run deployed both the Worker and Pages.

## #30806: subscriber admission off the synchronous funding path (partial)
Refs #30806

Before this change, every subscription-funded Worker request ran `reserveSynchronously(params, true)` before provider dispatch. That call is a locked primary transaction that allocates allowance and can also debit purchased credit. After this change, token-priced subscriber requests on the Worker take the Durable Object lease lane:

- **Admission:** reads funding capacity (purchased credit plus spendable allowance) inside the existing policy transaction and reuses that transaction's balance, revision and clock. It then leases that capacity in the organization's admission Durable Object, which serializes every in-flight estimate and runs the fused credential check.
- **After the provider responds:** `fundSubscriberInferenceCharge` funds the exact charge, allowance first, in one primary transaction. That transaction also reads the post-accounting capacity, so settling the gate needs no balance readback.
- **Alarm recovery:** replays the same `inference-gate:<requestId>` funding key, so a lease recovered after a lost settlement is never funded twice.
- **Zero-collected replay:** settlement now persists a durable zero-collected receipt (migration `0493`) while retaining the actual cost. Replaying an earlier unfunded turn cannot charge newly added funds or substitute a later lease estimate. Real database regression coverage exercises the replay boundary.

**How overspend is prevented**
- **Revision:** migration `0491` advances `organizations.balance_revision` on every platform allowance transaction. Allowance-only spend, grants, releases and expiry therefore all look newer to the gate.
- **Ceiling view:** the gate ledger records whether its ceiling is credit only or funding capacity (`balanceView: "funding"`).
  - At an equal revision, the funding view replaces a credit-only observation.
  - A newer revision always wins.
  - Two observations of the same view can only lower the ceiling.
  - A lease must carry the funding view exactly when its recovery accounting is `subscription_funding`.
  - The view field is additive, so a rollback reads the ledger conservatively.
- **Settlement:** caps the charge at the capacity available under the organization lock. Any remainder is reported as `uncollected_overage` and is never overdrawn, which matches the synchronous lane's policy.

**Unchanged:** affiliate-marked, flat-priced (pending video) and non-Worker subscriber requests keep the synchronous reservation.

**Folded micro-issues**
- **#30724 (done):** `requireInferenceApiKeyWithOrg` now resolves key, user and organization with one primary statement. Rejection classes, messages and order are unchanged. Timing is now a single `auth_identity` hop, and the latency script is updated to match.
- **#30715 (done):** as described above.
- **#30860, #30865, #30718, #30722 (already fixed on develop):**
  - #30860: the skip-mode memo write is gated in `steward-client.ts`.
  - #30865: the affiliate settlement publishes its transaction's balance and revision.
  - #30718: the policy statement already reads balance and revision.
  - #30722: the aged deferred refresh landed in #32834.
- **#30796 (remaining):** enabling `INFERENCE_STRONG_REVOCATION_ENABLED` in production needs the staging allow/deny/revoke canary and a normal release. This PR does not change it.

**Remaining**
- The policy-read and dispatch-policy transactions still run before dispatch. They were already there, so the zero pre-dispatch DB goal is still not met.
- Live Worker-to-Cerebras certification and the traces for the exact deployed revision.
- #30796, above.

## #22552: first-turn warming 503s (partial)
Refs #22552

Partial work landed in #32834, which added the session-start prewarm and the keepwarm conversation-object leg for Personal Shared rooms. This PR adds three things.

**Bounded join instead of an immediate warming 503**
- The conversation Durable Object's history hydration, the linked-character cache and the combined admission snapshot now wait up to 4s for their in-flight hydration before failing.
- Hydration that is slower than that, or that fails, still fails closed with the same retryable warming signal. The hydration keeps running under `waitUntil`.

**Keep-warm for rowless Personal Shared identities**
- A canonical personal turn records its verified owner in its conversation object. A forged projection, whose id is not derived from its account, is ignored.
- The room prewarm reports that organization, and the cron then warms the organization's rate-limit gate once per sweep. The namespaced id is never sent to a UUID lookup.

**Terminal transcript (#27685)**
- The startup/recovery restore (`hydrateInitialConversation`) wrote its server snapshot straight into the transcript, bypassing the local-turn overlay.
- A restore that landed while a send was absorbing warming retries evicted the pending turn. The next overlay capture then retired that turn for good, so the receipt had nothing left to re-anchor.
- The restore now merges through the same overlay reconciliation that history loads use. A regression test that uses the real `useChatSend`, `useDataLoaders` and `hydrateInitialConversation` reproduced the staging signature before the fix.

**Remaining**
- The 503-rate and time-to-first-reply re-measurement, with a sample size stated in advance.
- The canonical deployed staging run showing the anchored terminal turn.
- #27685 receipt and reconciliation counters: the app exposes no signal to count from yet.

4e:T105f,## Refs #25146: reversible Dedicated to Shared fallback, follow-up to #32856

#32856 added the fallback authority but only the connector paths used it. This PR covers the three code items that were left.

**1. Direct chat routes use the entitlement route.** A new module, `personal-direct-chat-route.ts`, gives every direct entry point for the `personal:` identity the same decision the connector paths already get from `resolvePersonalDedicatedRoute`. The entry points are:
- `GET /api/v1/eliza/personal`
- REST `conversations` list/create and `conversations/:id/messages` GET/POST
- `conversations/:id/messages/stream`
- `agents/:id/stream`
- `agents/:id/bridge` (`message.send`)

How each state is handled:
- **Access withdrawn:** the canonical conversation id is served from the interval's scoped journal, with the typed account-state provider. That provider now also reaches streaming, because `coordinateSharedStream` forwards `trustedAccountState`. Any other room returns 404, so the canonical, Dedicated and pre-upgrade history is never read.
- **Dedicated owns the conversation:** Shared surfaces return a typed 409 `personal_eliza_dedicated` with `activeAgentId`. Before, only the sealed canonical room refused; other room ids got through.
- **Recovery:** the journal is reconciled into the same Dedicated agent id before `/personal` or a surface hands that id back. If the coordinator is missing, the response is a retryable 503 `dedicated_reconciling`.

**2. Signed, expiring recovery links.** The pay action (`recoveryAction.link`) now carries an ES256 JWS. It is signed with the existing Cloud JWKS key (`lib/auth/jwks`), bound to org, user, interval id, generation and action kind, and expires after 72h. The public route `GET /api/v1/eliza/personal/recovery/:token` verifies the token and checks that the interval still exists. It then redirects to `/cloud/billing?organizationId=…&action=…`. The link carries no payment details and grants nothing: the billing page still requires sign-in. An expired link returns 410 and a bad token returns 400. The DO boundary parser admits only a well-formed resolver URL (no query, hash or credentials).

If the signing key is not configured, the link is left out and the gap is logged at error level. The `/cloud/billing` path still works.

**3. Group and shared-gateway traffic.** `resolvePersonalDedicatedTrafficAccess` refuses a withdrawn personal Dedicated with the typed state in four places:
- the connector gateway (`agent-gateway-router`, new reason `dedicated_access_withdrawn`)
- the Dedicated bridge dispatch in `agents/:id/bridge`
- the container control-plane `bridge`/`stream`
- group turns in `personal-shared/messages`

Group turns get a neutral `group_dedicated_access_paused` reply that does not disclose the owner's billing state. Once the owner's access is back, a group turn also triggers the recovery reconcile. Agents that are not cut-over personal Dedicated agents are unaffected.

## #29918: coherent sign-in, sign-out and recovery (partial)
Refs #29918

#32834 already re-landed the OAuth launch-origin fix and the bootstrap recovery-screen accessibility. This PR re-lands the remaining front-door pieces of the closed draft #30920 (credit: standujar), sized to what current develop can carry.

**`/join` sign-out now reaches a signed-out state.** The recorded failure was a sign-out that bounced back through `/login` into `/join` and restarted Dedicated setup. Two client paths could restore the session that had just ended:
- **Refresh race:** `StewardProviderRuntime`'s refresh-ahead loop wrote a rotated token unconditionally. A refresh that was in flight during sign-out therefore republished the session after it had been cleared. It now uses `replaceStoredStewardTokenIfCurrent`, a compare-and-swap against the token the refresh started from. A regression test fails without this change.
- **Surviving refresh cookie:** the login page silently restored a refresh cookie that survived sign-out. It no longer does this while the origin's explicit sign-out marker is set. The next explicit sign-in still clears the marker: through the login section, the SSO exchange, or, new here, a completed magic link.
- `JoinPage` now guards sign-out with a ref, so one activation sends exactly one hosted logout.

**Session revalidation after confirmation.** A join attempt is now bound to the account that started it. If the user signs out, switches account, or another tab signs in during the Dedicated quote or adoption review, the attempt is aborted before the billable POST and the user gets a retryable error. A token rotation for the same subject does not abort the attempt.

**Email completion.** Ordinary callback and cross-tab email completion use neutral Continue copy; only the app-authorization destination advertises app authorization.

**Bootstrap recovery screen.** The session is now persisted before it is published. A storage failure shows a recoverable error; before, it left the screen stuck on "submitting" with an unhandled rejection.

**#27241 (consolidated leaf):** phone and email/passkey validation messages now:
- appear next to the field that failed;
- set `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` on that field;
- keep `role=alert`;
- move focus to the invalid input.

## #16420: iOS native Cloud authentication (partial)
Refs #16420

This builds on the existing `ElizaCloudAuthSessionPlugin`, which uses ASWebAuthenticationSession with S256 PKCE and the claimed HTTPS callback `https://eliza.app/auth/callback`, and on the shared mobile PKCE client, which calls `app-auth/mobile/{config,token,ack}`.

- **Inactive until ACK:** the exchanged secret is staged in its own Keychain slot. The new secure-store key `session.cloud_mobile_pending` is added to the Swift, Kotlin and TS allowlists. The secret is promoted into the Keychain-backed Steward token store only after the server ACK. If the ACK response fails or is lost, the staged secret is retained for exact-key revocation: the server may already have committed the ACK. The prior active session remains untouched.
- **Exact recovery:** the ACK is recorded on the staged record before promotion. At launch, and before a new sign-in:
  - an acknowledged staged credential is promoted exactly once, without opening a second browser;
  - an unacknowledged one is revoked with its exact key before its local record is removed; a failed revoke preserves the record for retry.
- **Revocation:** sign-out and disconnect revoke any staged credential before the active credential or SSO session. Each exact-key `DELETE /api/v1/api-keys/current` must succeed before its local copy is removed. This prevents a staged credential left by failed activation from restoring a session after sign-out. Failed revocation preserves the corresponding credential for retry.
- **#30853 (consolidated):**
  - Native device-code sign-in now opens the owned Capacitor Browser instead of a renderer popup.
  - If persisting a claimed one-time CLI token fails, the client retries from memory instead of polling the consumed session again. A regression test fails without this change.
- **#28888 (consolidated):** the code-side items are already on develop:
  - idempotent callback ACK;
  - typed terminal failures that survive a cleanup failure;
  - removal of the stripped Android-only shell.

  Its remaining acceptance is device evidence (see below).

**Remaining (needs a device, signing and a deploy):**
- Clean-install runs on an iOS device and an Android device, including sign-in, cancel, cold relaunch, revocation, and the recovery paths.
- AASA/`webcredentials` verification for the signed team and bundle.
- Enabling the mobile config routes in staging and production.
- Cloud-only defaults for store builds.
- A product decision on iOS 16.0–17.4, which keeps the device-code fallback.

50:T218a,## #22930: billing hold after an underfunding refund or chargeback (Decision A)

Refs #22930

The owner approved option A. This PR builds on the existing clawback mechanics in `credits.ts` and `stripe-event.ts`, and on the `organization_payment_reversal_holds` table from migration 0479. It does not add a second hold model; migration **0492** extends that table.

- **Server-owned hold.** Sometimes a refund or dispute clawback cannot take the full reversed amount from the balance, leaving an unrecovered shortfall. In that case `clawbackCredits` inserts a `reversal_shortfall` hold **in the same SQL statement** as the clawback. That means a committed shortfall always has its hold. The hold records `shortfall_usd` and `outstanding_usd` and links to the clawback transaction.
- **Fail-closed admission.** While a hold is active, paid admission is refused with a typed `BillingHoldActiveError` (`code: billing_hold_active`). It extends `InsufficientCreditsError`, so every paid route that already maps a funding denial to HTTP 402 returns 402 for it. The hold is checked in these places:
  - inference admission (`admitOrganizationInference`, inside the policy transaction);
  - pre-spend reservations (`reserveCredits`, `reserveFlatUsageCredits`, `reserveAllowanceEligibleCredits`), which also block Plus/Pro allowance funding;
  - new containers (`createWithQuotaAndOptionalProjectIntent`, under the org lock);
  - Dedicated create, provision, resume, wake and upgrade (the existing `checkAgentCreditGate`, whose 402 body now carries `billing_hold_active` and `outstandingUsd`);
  - automatic resume.
- **Clearing the hold.**
  - Repayment: a Stripe top-up settles outstanding shortfalls oldest-first before anything else, recorded as a ledger debit. A hold is released once it is covered. The pay action also applies funds already in the balance.
  - `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`: releases the hold for that clawback, and returns any repayment already made toward it as a separate refund row that is not tagged with the payment intent, so later refund tallies stay correct.
  - Operator release still works (now keyed by hold id).
- **Lost disputes.** `charge.dispute.closed` with status lost no longer places a separate hold. The shortfall hold from `funds_withdrawn` already covers what is owed. Migration 0492 moves any still-owed legacy lost-dispute shortfall into a shortfall hold before it releases the old `chargeback_lost` holds.
- **API and UI.**
  - `GET /api/v1/billing/hold` returns a typed `{status: "clear"} | {status: "held", outstandingUsd, holds, payAction}`.
  - `POST /api/v1/billing/hold` (billing manager session only) applies the current balance.
  - The Billing tab shows a hold banner with **Pay $X**, which prefills the card top-up, and **Apply current balance**.

| Policy field | Decision |
|---|---|
| Trigger | Any refund (including partial, cumulative) or `funds_withdrawn` clawback that leaves `unrecovered_clawback_usd > 0`. A fully recovered reversal never holds. |
| Shortfall | Authoritative debt equal to the unrecovered amount. Credits that land later repay it first. |
| Blocked while held | Inference (credit- and allowance-funded), media/search/voice pre-spend, new containers, Dedicated create/provision/resume/wake/upgrade, automatic resume. |
| Running resources | Not force-stopped. They keep settling against existing funding and follow the existing unfunded-stop lifecycle. The credit balance is 0 while held. |
| Still available | Sign-in, billing history, reads/exports, cancellation/deletion, top-ups. |
| Restoration | Automatic once repayment covers the outstanding amount or Stripe reinstates the dispute. Operator release is kept for write-offs. |
| UI | Typed hold state, owed amount and pay action on the Billing tab. |
| Scope | Forward-only: shortfalls recorded before this deploy are not backfilled, except legacy lost-dispute shortfalls. |

51:T11fd,## Refs #25146: app UI for the Dedicated-to-Shared fallback

**Stacks on #32872** (`shaw/feat-fallback-direct-routes`). This branch is based on that one, so its diff includes #32872's server changes until #32872 merges. The UI change in this PR is the last commit and touches only `packages/ui`.

The server side (#32856, merged, and #32872) returns three typed signals to the app. This PR handles each one in chat.

**1. 409 `personal_eliza_dedicated`: switch to `activeAgentId` without losing the draft.**
`describePersonalRouteRefusal` (`api/personal-fallback.ts`) classifies the refusal. It walks the `cause` chain the same way the credit-gate classifier does. On a send or first-conversation create that gets this refusal, `useChatSend` does three things:
- It drops the refused turn's optimistic rows.
- It puts the text and attachments back in the composer, merged with anything typed since.
- It calls the route owner (`state/personal-fallback-route.ts`).

The route owner re-reads `/api/v1/eliza/personal`, which is the authority and also gives the trusted Dedicated `apiBase`. It then repoints the live chat in place through the existing `silentlyRepointToDedicated`. The logical `personal:` identity stays the same, drafts are not cleared, and `SWITCH_AGENT` is not dispatched. The lookup is single-flight and only repoints the identity that is still bound to the chat.

**2. `identity.accountState`: an honest banner in chat.**
`getPersonalSharedEliza` now parses `identity.accountState` and validates it at the boundary. If the field is present but malformed, the call throws; it is never treated as "no fallback". A new `PersonalAccountStateBanner` sits above the composer and uses the canonical `Banner` atom. It shows:
- the confirmed reason
- that Dedicated memory is unavailable until billing is restored
- the retention deadline, when one is set

The pay action ("Add credits" or "Restore subscription") opens the signed recovery link only when the link is unexpired and points at the same Cloud or a trusted control-plane host. Otherwise it opens `/cloud/billing`. `recovery_pending` shows an info state with no pay action. The banner re-resolves on mount and on every base change. When the server reports Dedicated again, it hands the chat back.

**3. 503 `dedicated_reconciling` (and `dedicated_fallback_pending`): a retryable state.**
The draft goes back to the composer. The banner shows a retryable state with a "Try again" button, and retries once on its own after `Retry-After`.

i18n: 14 new keys in `en.json`, with translations in all 7 other locales.

A story (`Chat/PersonalFallbackBanner`) covers the four banner states.

It covers reason, deadline, signed link vs expired-link fallback, no banner without a fallback, 503 → retry → handback to Dedicated, and malformed state rejected.

52:T124b,## Todos: match-and-confirm by visible content

Fixes #29690
Refs #29921

Todo lists and `CURRENT_TODOS` show only content, so update, complete, cancel, and delete had no way to target a todo across turns. This follows the owner's match-and-confirm decision and the review constraints in #29690.

- **Resolution happens in the store.** `target` (visible content or a close paraphrase) and `ref` are resolved inside `applyMutation`, under the scope advisory lock and within the entity/agent scope. The outcome is recorded in the mutation ledger. A retry with the same idempotency key replays the original outcome, even after the rename or delete removed the visible locator. Tests cover both the rename and the delete retry.
- **Ambiguity.** When several todos match, the reply is a clarification and nothing is mutated. Each candidate carries its state, creation date, and a stable `ref` derived from the row id, so no storage id is exposed and no positional index is used. A ref still selects the same row after other rows are added or renamed. Open todos are preferred over finished ones with the same content.
- **update** keeps `target` (which todo to change) separate from `content` (the new text).
- **clear** records a durable preview of exactly the rows it would remove.
  - The next call removes those rows, and only those, if the user's actual message is affirmative. Todos added after the preview survive.
  - Any other reply cancels, and a preview expires after 5 minutes.
  - The affirmative check reuses core's confirmation detector, now exported as `isAffirmativeConfirmationReply`. Model-supplied flags are never consulted.
  - The preview lives in the ledger rather than the runtime cache, which is per-turn in-memory on Shared edge hosts. Cross-room scope from #28006 is unchanged.
- **create** deduplicates in the store mutation under the scope lock, which also covers concurrent creates. When an open todo with the same content and parent exists, it returns that todo and asks. Only an affirmative reply to that question creates the second copy.
- **Compatibility.** Serialization, cutover remapping, and the scope assertions cover the new outcomes. Legacy mutation records and request digests are unchanged.

Verification, run one file at a time:
- `plugins/plugin-todos`: `vitest run test/todo-visible-content.real-db.test.ts` (new, 12 passed); `test/todos.real-db.test.ts` (26 passed); `test/todo-scope-convergence.real-db.test.ts` (4 passed); `src/actions/todo.test.ts` (74 passed); the two export tests passed; `bun run typecheck` passed; `biome check` is clean.
- `packages/testing`: `SCENARIO_USE_DETERMINISTIC_MODEL=1 … cli.ts run scenario-runner/test/scenarios --scenario deterministic-todos-actions` passed. The clear turn now previews, then confirms on "yes".
- `packages/cloud/shared`: `bun test src/lib/services/shared-runtime/shared-todos.test.ts` passed.

## Cloud sign-in stays on the originating host

Fixes #19214
Refs #29918

A signed-out visit to `cloud(-staging).eliza.app` (via `/join` or the app-mode entry) rode the SSO bridge to `(staging.)eliza.app/auth/bridge`. With no session there, the mint leg sent the user to that host's own `/login`, so sign-in finished on another host.

The bridge now only shares an existing session. When the mint host has none, it returns immediately to the paired app origin's `/login` with the sanitized `returnTo`, and sign-in completes on the host the user started on.

Unchanged: the fixed pair allowlist, the referrer gate, `sanitizeBridgeReturnTo`, the PKCE state and verifier binding, and the same-origin Steward OAuth redirect URI. The mint-intent memory existed only to resume after the other-host login, so it is removed.

A single automatic hop to the mint host remains for signed-out visitors. Steward cookies are host-only, so the app origin cannot tell in advance whether the mint host holds a session to share. No credential is entered on the mint host.

Verification (`packages/ui`, one file at a time):
- `SsoBridgeRoute.test.tsx` passed (26). The new case covers production and staging, asserting a return to the app host's `/login?returnTo=/chat` with no fetch and no retained state. The open-redirect case collapses to the app host's `/login?returnTo=/`.
- `AppModeEntryRoute.sso.test.tsx` (5), `JoinPage.sso-handoff.test.tsx` (5), and `login-page.same-origin.test.tsx` (4) passed.
- `bun run typecheck` passed; `biome check` is clean on the changed files.

Not run: the repo-wide `bun run verify`, and the live staging check from #19214 (start and finish on cloud-staging with one tab), which needs a deployed build.

55:T1256,A correct Notes read reply containing a standalone `Saved:` timestamp can trigger the completed-write detector when a preceding body sentence ends with a period. The guard then asks for an applied mutation receipt that cannot exist for a read, unnecessarily rewrites the reply, and ultimately rejects it.

Recognize only exact standalone Saved/Created/Updated field values grounded in a current successful internal Notes read's structured host display. Validate the canonical instant, timezone, source, record binding and snapshot revision; later writes, stale/conflicting reads, failed actions and pending work do not gain an exemption. Scan every remaining assertion unchanged and retain the original reply bytes for effect-receipt matching. Mixed delivered navigation plus reads is covered, including evaluator applied status. The final visible boundary rechecks authorized current results before rewriting, without adding a model call or warning for an already grounded observation.

No generic “Saved” removal, blanket read-only/evaluator-none bypass, forged write receipt, Notes import, output-schema change, model setting, source projection or record mutation. Real write claims before/after metadata still require their existing proof. This patch is independent of the separate Notes canonical-content descriptor PR #32882.

Validation: the actual original case7 reply/result rejected before and passes both planned and visible egress after, with byte-identical reply/results and zero model calls or warnings. 123 tests across six guard/recovery/navigation suites passed on this independent branch; assistant typecheck, lint and diff checks passed. Independent review passed. Full repository verification and app build passed in the integrated checkout containing source commit `078aec0b2d8`, not as an independent-branch full-suite claim.

A later integrated live smoke at `078aec0b2d8` returned the correct saved timestamp using only NOTES_LIST and three H/P/E calls: 34,622 input tokens, 12,288 cached input as a subset, 966 output, 3,856 ms whole turn. Separate background: 7,498 input, zero cached, 119 output, 1,024 ms. All eleven notes and Calendar were unchanged.

Important limitation: that live reply quoted its body, changing the punctuation immediately before Saved. The unchanged old detector returns false for the new reply and true for the original failure. Therefore the live smoke does **not** establish that this patch prevented recovery or improved speed. The unchanged-original offline regression is the discriminating proof. Earlier failures remain preserved; no universal timestamp/prose reliability or all-campaign pass is claimed.

56:T183b,Refs #30744, #30825

I found three defects while verifying the merged desktop fixes on a packaged macOS build (unsigned Electrobun build from origin/develop `279e585dd84`, port 31337 held by a dummy listener). One commit each.

## 1. Local backups fail on every desktop and standalone agent (`fix(agent)`)

`POST /api/backups` returns HTTP 500 even for a tiny PGlite agent:
- The local backup path calls `createKmsClient()` with no options.
- Outside tests, that resolves to the Steward backend. A local agent never configures Steward.
- The snapshot manifest is built, then encryption throws:

```
Info  [agent-backup] Snapshot manifest created (database=pglite-dump, mediaFiles=0, vaultFiles=0, stateFiles=32)
Error [agent-backup] Local backup failed (err=ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=steward requires steward.{baseUrl, tokenProvider})
```

The suites missed it because `NODE_ENV=test` selects the in-memory KMS. The v1 envelope and the streamed v2 key wrap are both affected.

**Fix:** local backups now seal with the local KMS adapter, rooted in the same OS keychain master key as the agent vault (or `ELIZA_VAULT_PASSPHRASE`).
- An explicit `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND`, `ELIZA_LOCAL_MODE`, `ELIZA_LOCAL_ROOT_KEY`, or a test environment keeps the current resolution.
- An unavailable master key is surfaced and not cached.
- Limits and backup contents are unchanged.

## 2. Tray, menu, shortcut and deep-link callbacks crash the packaged app (`fix(desktop)`)

Bun 1.4.2 (the bundled runtime) passes `FFIType.cstring` JSCallback arguments as JS strings. Electrobun 1.18.1 converts each one with `new CString(arg)`, which throws `TypeError: ptr must be a number`. I reproduced this with a standalone Bun FFI callback.

On the packaged app:
- Every `webviewEvent` / `eventBridgeHandler` conversion logs this error and the event is dropped.
- The tray, application-menu, context-menu, global-shortcut, URL-open (deep link) and MIME callbacks have no try/catch. The first tray action or Cmd+Q ended in `Uncaught exception in worker` and killed the main process.
- The embedded agent was left orphaned (ppid 1) on port 31339.

**Fix:** the desktop build now rewrites these callback conversions in the shared Electrobun source and in every downloaded platform core (`dist-*`). The rewrite sits next to the existing RPC socket hardening, and the packaging step repackages when the downloaded core changes. The helper accepts strings, null, or raw pointers. If the upstream code shape changes, the build fails.

After the fix, the same tray and menu Quit actions run the normal shutdown: `App quitting (explicit-quit)`, agent stopped, no crash.

## 3. A returning packaged profile never leaves `starting-runtime` (`fix(desktop)`)

The packaged desktop embeds the app server host (`packages/app/src/runtime/startup-state.ts`). Its `/api/health` reports `startup.phase` as `runtime-ready`, `features-starting`, `ready` or `degraded`, all projected as agent state `running`. Only the legacy agent host reports `running`.

Two checks required the phase to be exactly `running`:
- The shell's launch orchestrator (`classifyPhase`).
- The renderer's `isRuntimeReadyFromBootProgress` and `isRuntimeReadyFromLaunchProgress`.

So with a configured profile:
- The launch snapshot stayed at `agent-api-ready` (`boot.runtimePhase: "ready"`).
- The renderer stayed in `starting-runtime` with an empty pill window, even though `/api/status` said `running`.

Fresh profiles were unaffected because first-run gating runs first. Dev and web builds were unaffected because they don't use the desktop RPC.

**Fix:** both checks accept every phase the hosts project as running.

## Verification

Packaged app, unsigned, 31337 occupied by a dummy listener. The embedded API moved to 31339 in every run.

| Fresh state dir | Backend `first-run/status complete:false`, `status not_started (awaiting-onboarding)`. Renderer: `restoring-session → resolving-target → polling-backend → first-run-required`, showing "Let's get you signed in / Sign in to Eliza Cloud" with a gated composer. Never ready. |
| Returning profile, before fix 3 | `/api/status running`, but the renderer stayed in `starting-runtime` with an empty body for more than 4 minutes. |
| Returning profile, after fix 3 | `starting-runtime → hydrating → ready`. `meta.firstRunComplete` preserved and `first-run/status complete:true`. |
| Backup round trip, packaged app after fix 1 | Created conversation `PACKAGED-BACKUP-MARKER`, then `POST /api/backups` (200, 82 MB), then deleted it, then `POST /api/backups/restore` (`{"restored":true,"requiresRestart":true}`). After relaunch the marker is back. |

57:T117a,## Routine delivery with no conversation (Refs #31022)

A fresh agent with no conversation dropped fired in-app routines, but the scheduler still recorded them as delivered. Two things caused it:

- The personal-assistant `in_app` dispatcher counted an `assistant` event emit as delivery. That emit is fire-and-forget.
- The server's autonomy router, `routeAutonomyTextToUser`, returned silently when no conversation existed.

### Fix
This uses option (a): resolve or create the owner's canonical conversation, then persist durably.

- **core**: Adds the internal send source `owner_chat` (`MESSAGE_SOURCE_OWNER_CHAT`). `sendMessageToTarget` now throws a typed `ElizaError` with the code `SEND_HANDLER_NOT_FOUND` when a source has no handler. The message text is unchanged.
- **agent**: Registers an `owner_chat` handler that picks the owner's canonical conversation:
  - It uses the active conversation, else the most recent one. It never picks a wallet-scoped waifu conversation.
  - If none exists, `ensureOwnerConversation` creates one through the same room setup as `POST /api/conversations`, and concurrent creations share one result.

  The handler persists the message before it resolves. A `deliveryIdempotencyKey` derives the message id, so a replay returns the stored row instead of writing a second one.
- **personal-assistant**: `in_app` dispatch now awaits the durable history write.
  - Only chat history or the notification inbox count as delivery. The event bus does not.
  - The emitted event carries the persisted `roomId`, so the autonomy router does not store a second copy.
  - If the history write fails, the dispatch returns a typed, retryable `transport_error`.
  - A headless host has no `owner_chat` handler and no notifier, so it gets `disconnected` and the task is never marked delivered.
  - `owner_chat` is added to the internal-source set in `delivery-binding.ts`, which checks for missing sources at compile time.

### Test
`packages/agent/test/scheduled-routine-no-conversation-delivery.test.ts` runs on a real SQLite runtime with the scheduling plugin, the PA production dispatcher, the agent event bus and the HTTP server:
- **Headless** (no server): firing the routine records `lastDispatchResult` `{ok:false, reason:"disconnected"}`, and the fire result is not `fired`. On develop this assertion fails, because the fire result is `fired`.
- **Server running, no conversation, no client**: the routine fires. When a client connects, `GET /api/conversations` returns exactly one conversation, and its room matches `ownerChatRoomId` in the scheduler record. Its history holds the routine exactly once, under the `ownerChatMessageId` the scheduler recorded.
- **Replay** of the same occurrence: dispatch returns the same message id, with no new row and no new conversation.

58:T42e,An acknowledged Blooio/Twilio message held during Shared-to-Dedicated cutover could be lost after a transient retry failure or a partial Redis write. A slow redelivery could also outlive its lease and be executed by another replica.

59:T116e,Refs #31017. This is the follow-up to #32870. It fixes the remaining action-retrieval problems at their source.

## (b) Incidental domain words no longer load whole families

In `convo.echo-self-test`, Stage 1 selects `ECHO_TEST` for "Please echo this message back to me: hello world". The `world` token matched the MESSAGE family's `world` context, so the pending-intent bootstrap loaded nine `MESSAGE_*` tools. `pendingActionContexts` in `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/services/message/action-surface.ts` now requires a stronger signal when a clause is already claimed by a selected action (its verb names that action, e.g. `echo` names `ECHO_TEST`). An incidental domain word in that clause counts only if the clause also names one of the domain's operations. Otherwise the family stays behind `DISCOVER_ACTIONS`. Unclaimed clauses keep the existing inference, including #32835's pending programming-work cases. Declared contexts and explicit discovery are unchanged.

## (a) Promoted children no longer repeat the parent description

`promoteSubactionsToActions` used to compose each virtual's description as `${parent.description} — subaction = x`. A virtual now states only its operation, for example `MESSAGE operation "edit".` plus any authored override. The promotion marker carries the umbrella description. `buildPlannerToolsFromActions` and `buildPlannerToolsFromTieredActions` state it once per tool list: on the family's first exposed operation, with later operations referencing that tool. When the umbrella tool is already on the wire they add nothing. Canonical alias contracts (`planned-tool.ts`) and sub-planner value guides reconstruct the exact prior composed text from the marker. No context is truncated or summarized; the umbrella text is stated once rather than N times.

5a:T857,## Problem
`cloud / e2e-tests` on develop failed 3× in a row at e1de9b114d (run 36392903797). Each time the worker "died" 14–20 s into the suite on a different route (MCP, discord heartbeat, advertising media): a plain-text 500 `Error: Network connection lost.` from Miniflare's entry worker, or wrangler exiting with an empty `✘ [ERROR]`. No `packages/cloud` code changed between the last green run and this SHA.

## Root cause
`wrangler dev` forwards every request ProxyWorker → UserWorker over a pooled keep-alive connection; both kj endpoints close idle sockets after 5 s, so a request written at that boundary never reaches the Worker. Wrangler only retries GET/HEAD, and the test client's `Connection: close` cannot reach that hop (cloudflare/workers-sdk#14641). The race is always present (passing runs also log kj `Broken pipe`) and becomes fatal under runner load. Reproduced on stock wrangler 4.116.0 with a hello-world Worker in a Linux container: 673–1286 of 1920 timed requests failed, then the server died with the same empty `[ERROR]`.

## Fix
Test lanes (`CLOUD_E2E=1`, `NODE_ENV=test`, no custom wrangler args) launch the Worker through `wrangler-direct-dev.ts`. It keeps wrangler's config, bundler and Miniflare runtime (`unstable_DevEnv`), but serves the public port from a Node listener that never idles out clients and forwards each request to the UserWorker over a fresh connection with the same original-URL and shared-secret headers. It never replays requests and rejects any unrecognized flag. Local dev is unchanged.

No assertions were weakened, no tests were skipped, and no retries were added.

## Verification
- Linux A/B burst test: 0/1920 failures with the launcher vs 673 and 1286 on stock wrangler. JSON, 100 KB and 5 MB bodies, multiple set-cookies, SSE and WebSocket upgrades behave the same as stock.
- `bun run test:cloud:e2e` against local Postgres, run twice: 18/18 files, 512 pass / 19 skip, identical to the baseline.
- `bun run test:cloud:integration`: 511 pass, 0 fail. No leftover workerd processes.
- biome clean.

5c:Td2a,## Fixes #32880

`projectFinalizedTranscriptCommitments` / `commitmentRecordsFromTranscript` resolved `tomorrow`, weekdays, and explicit dates on the UTC calendar and pinned deadlines at 17:00 UTC. An evening promise in a zone behind UTC was a day late, and it disagreed with the zone-aware meeting-ghost row (#31249 / #32837) written by the same finalized event.

**Changes (plugin-personal-assistant)**
- `transcript-hook.ts`: resolves the owner's effective zone at the transcript instant with `resolveOwnerTimeZone`. This is the same resolver and instant the meeting-ghost consumer uses. The zone is passed into the extractor.
- `commitments/ledger.ts`: `extractCommitmentLedgerRecords` accepts an optional `timeZone` and uses the shared `lifeops/time` local-day helpers (`getZonedDateParts`, `addDaysToLocalDate`, `getWeekdayForLocalDate`, `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`) for explicit dates, `tomorrow`, weekdays, and the 17:00 local wall time. Callers that pass no zone (chat, sent mail) keep UTC behavior. An impossible explicit date such as `2026-02-30` now gives no deadline instead of rolling into the next month.
- `meeting-ghost/index.ts`: aligns the two paths so one spoken commitment never persists as two rows with conflicting deadlines. The two writers used different source keys (`transcriptId:segmentId` vs `meetingId:commitmentId`), so both rows persisted.
  - Relative days now count from the utterance instant (meeting start + `segment.startMs`), which matches the projection's `observedAt`.
  - The ghost analyzer no longer writes a ledger row for the owner's own first-person promise when the transcript projection writes that segment. Named commitments ("Ava will…") spoken by the owner, and every other speaker's commitments, still come from the ghost path. Approvals and calendar intents are unchanged.

**Regression** (`test/meeting-ghost.integration.test.ts`, real PGlite runtime through `handleMeetingTranscriptFinalized`):
- The issue's exact repro: America/Los_Angeles, Monday 2026-09-14 17:30, "I will send the report tomorrow." gives `transcript-local-day:seg-1` with `dueAt` `2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z` and `observedAt` `2026-09-15T00:30:00.000Z`. Exactly one row is persisted per spoken commitment, and the ghost-sourced row for another speaker agrees on the local day.
- East of UTC: Asia/Tokyo, Tuesday 08:00 JST (still Monday in UTC). "on Wednesday" and "tomorrow" both give `2026-09-16T08:00:00.000Z`.
- Both new cases fail on the pre-fix source (2 failed / 3 passed) and pass with the fix.

5e:T1e98,# Relates to

#32905 added the durable cutover hold (#22934), and it merged today. `drainCutoverHolds` promises that "a per-hold lease keeps replicas from running the same turn concurrently", but that only holds while a redelivery finishes within one lease period.

`packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/cutover-hold.ts` on develop does three things:

- It takes `SET NX EX 180` on `webhook:cutover-hold-lease:<dedupKey>`.
- It awaits `handlers.redeliver(held)`. That is `redeliverHeldWebhook` → `processMessage` → `sendPersonalSharedReply`, which runs a full Personal Shared turn: up to `PERSONAL_SHARED_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS = 15 * 60_000` per attempt (`webhook-handler.ts:78`), with up to `PERSONAL_SHARED_ATTEMPTS = 3` attempts.
- It always runs `finally { await redis.del(leaseKey) }`.

The hold stays due in `webhook:cutover-holds` while it is being redelivered, and every replica polls every 2 s (`index.ts:256`, `CUTOVER_HOLD_DRAIN_INTERVAL_MS = 2_000`). `draining` only stops new drains, so during a rolling deploy the old pod keeps its in-flight redelivery while the new pod polls. So:

1. **Duplicate turn:** once a redelivery runs past 180 s, another replica re-acquires the lease and delivers the same held turn again.
2. **Lease stolen on release:** the first drainer's unconditional `del` then deletes the second drainer's lease, so a third can join.

`redeliverHeldWebhook` calls `processMessage` directly, so the ingress-side processing claim does not protect this path. The user receives the reply twice. On private Blooio/Twilio replies the gateway has no egress lease.

# Change

`drainCutoverHolds` (`cutover-hold.ts`):

- The lease value is a per-drain `randomUUID()` token.
  - Before, it was `String(now)`, and the Redis adapters JSON-parse reads, so that value came back as a number.
- While the redelivery runs, the lease is renewed every third of its period. Renewal stops, with a warning, if the lease is no longer ours. A failed renewal is logged and retried on the next tick.
- The lease is released only if we still own it.
- `options.leaseSeconds` (default 180, unchanged) lets tests outlive a lease in real time.
- **Crash recovery is unchanged:** a dead drainer's lease still lapses within one period.
- Renewal and release are atomic compare-and-act operations: `GatewayRedis.expireIfEquals` / `delIfEquals`, each a single server-side Lua script on the ioredis, ioredis-mock and Upstash adapters.
  - A GET-then-act check (the first revision) left a one-round-trip gap in which the lease could change hands. Thanks @ss251.
  - The hand-written `MemoryRedis` fake in `personal-private-egress.test.ts` implements the two methods too.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop`.
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [ ] Updated relevant documentation.

This used Ubuntu 24.04 (WSL2), Bun 1.4.2 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` on develop `3c2fbd0a5e`.

**Reproduced on develop (unmodified).**
- A throwaway test (not committed) drove the real `handleWebhook` → hold → `drainCutoverHolds` → `redeliverHeldWebhook` path against a local Cloud route, with the Blooio provider API substituted.
- The Cloud route deletes the lease key while the attested turn runs, to stand in for the 180 s lease lapsing.
- A second drainer then delivered the same turn:

```
first {"delivered":1,...} second {"delivered":1,...} cloudTurns 2 providerSends 2
(fail) develop: a lapsed lease lets a second replica deliver the same held turn
```

**New tests** in `__tests__/cutover-hold.test.ts` use the same real handler, drainer and Redis path as the existing cutover tests (in-memory Redis; only the Blooio API is substituted):

- *a redelivery that outlives one lease period is not run again by another replica*:
  - Setup: lease is 1 s, the attested turn takes 2.5 s, and a second drainer runs at 1.5 s.
  - Expected: the second drain is a no-op, and there is exactly 1 new Cloud turn, 1 provider send, and ledger `delivered`.
- *a drainer releases only its own lease*:
  - Setup: another replica's token replaces the lease mid-turn.
  - Expected: finishing leaves it intact. **Fails on develop** (`Expected: "other-replica-lease"`, the lease was deleted).

A new contract test runs `delIfEquals` / `expireIfEquals` through the in-memory adapter, which executes the real Lua scripts. It checks that a lost lease cannot extend or delete another owner's lease, while the owner can do both.

| Sabotage | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Renewal disabled | "outlives one lease period" fails (a second delivery happens); the others pass |
| Unconditional `del(leaseKey)` restored | "releases only its own lease" fails; the others pass |

60:T2665,# Relates to

#30330 (merged) shipped four behaviour lines whose tests pass with the lines
deleted; attentionhead's review there documented this. #30376 repaired those
tests and was closed in the 09-26 triage because its files moved. This is the
fresh PR against the current layout that the close note invited.

The old test files (`action-tiering.test.ts`, 12-case
`client-cloud-handoff-target.test.ts`) were removed on 09-23 in `55f4f25689`.
This PR does not restore them. It adds **one case per guard, four in total**,
and nothing else: no shared helpers, no matrix, and no `vi.mock`/`vi.fn`/`vi.spyOn`.

# Change

Test-only. Two new files; no source is touched.

| Guard on develop (`4a657d88e2`) | What it protects | Case |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `left.result.rank - right.result.rank \|\|` in `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/runtime/action-tiering.ts:132` | For parents whose relevance saturates at 1, the planner sees retrieval's order, not alphabetical order. Retrieval's own comments cite live trajectories where a saturated tie decided the surface. | `orders a saturated tie by retrieval rank, not by name` |
| `if (!dedicatedAgentId && sourceIsSharedAdapter) return null;` in `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts` | A shared source with no provisioned target is never switched in place, even when an older control plane omits `executionTier`. | `refuses a shared source with no dedicated target, …` |
| `if (agent.execution_tier === "shared") return null;` | A running row that is shared-tier is never a migration target. | `refuses a running target whose control-plane row is shared-tier` |
| `if (isDirectCloudSharedAgentBase(base) && !isLocalDedicatedProxy) return null;` | A target that advertises the shared REST adapter as its runtime is never switched onto. | `refuses a target that advertises the shared REST adapter as its runtime` |

How the cases are built:

- **Tiering.** The case drives the real `buildActionCatalog`, then
  `retrieveActions`, then `tierActionResults`. It first asserts the input is
  a genuine saturated tie ordered against the alphabet: both scores are 1 and
  WEATHER ranks above ARCHIVE. Then it asserts the tiered order keeps that
  ranking.
- **Handoff.** The cases run the real `ElizaClient.startCloudAgentHandoff`
  against a loopback HTTP server. The server answers the agent detail route,
  runtime health, the shared conversation, and import. So every route a
  completed switch needs is available, and the guard is the only thing
  preventing a switch. Each case asserts:
  - the handoff times out;
  - `onSwitch` is never called;
  - the target runtime is never contacted (no health probe, no import).
- **Deciding rejections without a clock race.** This was the 09-14 backlog
  point on #30376. Polls are sequential, so three detail reads prove two
  handled refusals. A slow runner can only make the test fail; it cannot make
  it pass without the guard. The short-circuit case is the deliberate
  negative: it refuses before any read, so it asserts zero reads across the
  whole budget. No fake timers are needed, because the handoff now accepts
  `intervalMs` and `timeoutMs`.

- [x] Targets `develop`; branched from `origin/develop` at `4a657d88e2`.

- [x] Documentation: N/A. Only test files change.

Test-only gate:

- **Realistic regression prevented.** Deleting any one of the four lines
  leaves every suite on develop green. Two concrete effects: the planner's
  parent order for a saturated tie turns alphabetical, and a cloud handoff
  switches the app onto a base that cannot host a dedicated runtime, which the
  user sees as a dead post-handoff chat.
- **Observing consumer.** For tiering, the tier-A parent order feeds
  `services/message/action-surface.ts` and the planner prompt. For the guards,
  `startCloudAgentHandoff` decides the base the app switches to after
  onboarding and upgrade.
- **Why existing coverage does not own it.** The only surviving tiering test
  (`tasks-issue-similes.test.ts`) never produces a tied score. The cloud
  end-to-end specs cover "no container reachable, so stay" and the success
  switch; neither covers "a shared runtime is reachable, so refuse it". The
  mutation results below demonstrate the gap rather than asserting it.

Updates `drizzle-orm` from 0.44.7 to 0.45.3
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/releases">drizzle-orm's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>0.45.3</h2>
<h2>New Netlify DB Driver</h2>
<blockquote>
<p>Note: The Netlify DB driver is developed and maintained by the Netlify team.</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Installation:</strong></p>
<pre lang="bash"><code>npm i @netlify/db
</code></pre>
<p><strong>Usage example:</strong></p>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';
<p>// reads NETLIFY_DB_URL and NETLIFY_DB_DRIVER env vars
const db = drizzle();</p>
<p>const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre></p>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';

const db = drizzle(process.env.DATABASE_URL);

const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre>
<pre lang="typescript"><code>import { drizzle } from 'drizzle-orm/netlify-db';

// Explicit client — consumer controls the driver
const db = drizzle({ client: netlifyDbClient });

const result = await db.execute('select 1');
</code></pre>
<h2>0.45.2</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed <code>sql.identifier()</code>, <code>sql.as()</code> escaping issues. Previously all the values passed to this functions were not properly escaped
causing a possible SQL Injection (CWE-89) vulnerability</li>
</ul>
<p>Thanks to <a href="https://github.com/EthanKim88"><code>@​EthanKim88</code></a>, <a href="https://github.com/0x90sh"><code>@​0x90sh</code></a> and <a href="https://github.com/wgoodall01"><code>@​wgoodall01</code></a> for reaching out to us with a reproduction and suggested fix</p>
<h2>0.45.1</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions breaking in environments with forbidden <code>require()</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/issues/5107">#5107</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>0.45.0</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed pg-native Pool detection in node-postgres transactions</li>
<li>Allowed subqueries in select fields</li>
<li>Updated typo algorythm =&gt; algorithm</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

<details>

<blockquote>
<h2>v10.0.12</h2>

<h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
<ul>
<li>settle every send on a connection error, back off pool requeues, turn a bare CR into CRLF, bound fetch, honour requireTLS (<a href="https://github.com/nodemailer/nodemailer/commit/63ccd66c894bbaac18c82084ee385bf6488226cf">63ccd66</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h2>v10.0.11</h2>

<li><a href="https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/commit/9bd8d464e6328c567c2dbcd8fdd97...

_Description has been truncated_62:Td09,# Relates to

The evaluator could finish a request without an answer even when its decision state required one, causing a reply-writing planner call and another evaluation.

# Change

Require `messageToUser` in the stable output schema and use an empty string for continuation or approval of an existing reply. Existing nonempty-answer checks and legacy parsing stay intact. No model defaults, tool execution rules or restoration behavior change.

# Testing

Head `94363faa5cd` (draft commit `a96b51848a8` + merge of `origin/develop` at `1f8f5278c16`, no conflicts), Bun 1.4.2 / Node 24.15.0:

- `plugins/plugin-assistant`: `vitest run` 436 files / 5870 tests passed (evaluator suites: 4 files / 91 tests); `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `biome check src` exit 0 (pre-existing warnings only).
- `convo.context-hygiene` scenario (pr-deterministic lane, zero-key fixtures) passed locally.
- Full hosted CI (workflow_dispatch) run 36387399629 on `94363faa5cd`: success, 29 jobs, including Zero-Key scenario runner.

- [x] Targets `develop`; merged current `origin/develop` cleanly.
- [ ] `bun run verify` not run locally; covered by the hosted CI run above.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation: N/A - internal evaluator output contract; prompt instructions updated alongside schema.

64:T12d3,## #31316: Cloud Worker startup CPU (Refs #29024)

Fixes #31316

### Why it regressed
#31317 moved the Cloud logger and two Shared runtime helpers onto core leaf exports. Then 1047e6d0096 collapsed core's `exports` map to `.` only, so those imports went back to the full `@elizaos/core` root. That commit consolidated the export surface; it did not say the leaves were wrong. Core now has explicit leaf subpaths again, but these files never moved back to them.

The metafile showed a second path that #31317 never removed. `db/schemas/index.ts` imports the table objects from the `@elizaos/plugin-sql` root, and that root imports the core barrel. So fixing only the logger would have left the core barrel in startup. Nine eagerly loaded modules imported the core root. This PR removes all of them.

### Change
- **core**: added the `./security/redact`, `./network/ssrf`, `./types/primitives` and `./utils/string-to-uuid` subpaths.
  - `stringToUuid` moved into its own leaf. `utils.ts` re-exports it.
  - `mintInferenceTraceId` moved into the existing `inference-trace` leaf. `inference-timing.ts` re-exports it.
  - Each function still has one implementation and the root barrel's API is unchanged. The leaves don't import the runtime barrel, as `packages/core/AGENTS.md` requires.
- **plugin-sql**: added a `./schema` entry: a tsup entry plus an export map entry. `src/schema.ts` now also exports its tables by name. It imports only drizzle and core types.
- **cloud/shared**: moved the eager importers to leaves:
  - `logger.ts`, `shared-runtime-channel.ts` and `shared-runtime-history-policy.ts` (the three from the issue)
  - `http-telemetry.ts`, `anonymous-sessions.ts`, `onboarding-coordinator-transport.ts` and `shared-runtime-errors.ts`
  - `db/schemas/index.ts`, which now imports from `@elizaos/plugin-sql/schema`

**What stays the same after 1047e6d0096.** There is still a single cloud logger module and a single redaction policy: `isSensitiveKeyName` and `redactLogArgs` come from the same core `security/redact` module. That module holds no configurable state, only a regex cache. `ElizaError` identity also holds across copies, because `instanceof` checks a shared brand. The deferred alarm-recovery and X-credential imports from #31317 are unchanged.

### Measurements
Same tree and toolchain, from `wrangler deploy --env production --dry-run` (the `check:worker-bundle` script) with `--metafile`. "Eager" means statically reachable from `src/index.ts`, which is what esbuild evaluates at Worker startup.

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Total upload (whole bundle) | 27959.21 KiB / gzip 6903.47 KiB | 27965.13 KiB / gzip 6905.95 KiB |
| Eager modules at startup | 1159 | **483** |
| Eager input bytes | 9.34 MB | **3.11 MB** |
| Core modules evaluated at startup | 487 (4762 KiB) | 0 dist barrel modules; only leaf sources |
| plugin-sql evaluated at startup | 676 KiB (adapter runtime) | 79 KiB (tables only) |
| Core root barrel eager | yes | **no** |

Total bundle size is flat because the heavy code still ships and loads lazily on demand. The gain is in what the Worker evaluates when it starts.

**Startup profile.** I ran `wrangler check startup --worker <prebuilt bundle>` three times on each bundle, interleaved:

| | run 1 | run 2 | run 3 | median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| before, active CPU (ms) | 11716 | 10841 | 13546 | **11716** |
| after, active CPU (ms) | 5638 | 8353 | 5697 | **5697** |
| before, script (ms) | 8955 | 8638 | 10618 | 8955 |
| after, script (ms) | 4962 | 4788 | 4366 | 4788 |

The machine's load average was 150–260 during these runs, so the absolute numbers are far above real Cloudflare timings. Only the relative drop (about 50% active CPU, about 47% script time) means anything. This is local evidence only: canonical staging deployment and the separate Dedicated latency measurement from the issue's acceptance criteria are still pending.

68:T8a3,When a configured remote backend's startup status probe fails, the startup flow skipped the readiness wait and announced `AGENT_RUNNING`. This follow-up to #32862 treats an unavailable status as unproven readiness: it uses the existing bounded polling path, recovers when the backend starts, and presents the existing timeout state if it stays unavailable.

Refs #30744. This fixes the reproduced status-probe failure only. It does not claim packaged macOS or microphone acceptance.

**Status probe classification** (`readRemoteRuntimeState` now uses the existing `runStartupProbe`):
- `running`: immediate-ready path (unchanged).
- HTTP 404, meaning the backend does not expose `/api/status`: keeps the previous immediate-ready path. This addresses @mtcow's review point that self-hosted backends without the endpoint must not regress into `agent-timeout`.
- Transport or other failure, or a response with no `state`: enters the bounded readiness loop.
- Any other reported state: enters the readiness loop (unchanged).

**Tests** (`startup-embedded-first-run.test.tsx`):
- The retry test now asserts that `getStatus` is called twice.
- New coverage for the 404 carve-out.
- New coverage for the persistent-failure timeout.

**Verification** at `2058d2c6e5f` (fix `c573c3d3cfc`, with current develop merged cleanly):
- Focused startup suite: 9/9 passed; with the Android cloud-auth suite, 15/15. Ran with a temporary config override (`pool: threads`, `execArgv: []`) because fork workers stall on this host.
- `packages/ui` typecheck: passed.
- Biome check on `src scripts`: passed. No errors; the warnings are pre-existing and in other files.

69:T7ac,`cloud / e2e-tests` failed on all four attempts of develop `e1de9b114d` (run 36392903797). `cloud / integration-tests` fails intermittently the same way (jobs 108789267585, 108762904596). Each time a different POST or PATCH got a plain-text 500 `Error: Network connection lost.` from Miniflare's `entry.worker.js`.

**Root cause (upstream, still open):** cloudflare/workers-sdk#14641. Wrangler dev's ProxyWorker reuses a pooled connection to the UserWorker at the instant KJ's 5s idle keep-alive timeout closes it. The server closes that idle socket before reading the new request, so the request never reaches the Worker. Our wrangler logs show no reload, crash or OOM around the drops. The existing `Connection: close` only covers the client→wrangler hop, and the helper retried only GET and HEAD.

**Change** (e2e helper only, no product code):
- Replay any method **once**, only when the response matches Miniflare's exact ProxyWorker `Network connection lost` stack (local target, 500, `text/plain`, non-JSON).
- Every other mutation failure stays fail-closed. That includes workerd's plain `Internal Server Error`, a bare "Network connection lost" text, and JSON problems.
- If a replayed mutation had actually applied, it surfaces as a conflict or duplicate, not a false pass. Each replay is logged with `workers-sdk#14641`.
- GET/HEAD recycle retries are unchanged.

The owner chose this over rewriting the harness to bypass the ProxyWorker (`unstable_startWorker` plus direct UserWorker dispatch). That rewrite is the proper removal of the race and is tracked as a follow-up.

**Tests:** `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/e2e-api-client-retry.test.ts`, 11/11 passing locally. It covers a single replay for POST, PATCH and DELETE on the exact drop, at most one replay, and no replay for the other failures. biome check is clean. Repeated dispatched Cloud Tests runs are the e2e verification.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)6a:T36c3,# Relates to

Every Android and iOS build of `develop` fails at `packages/agent build:mobile` (the first step of `build:android:cloud`, `build:android:cloud:debug` and `.github/workflows/android-build.yml`):

